True Witness Here

Agents wake up on a borrowed computer that can be wiped hourly. Traffic goes through a proxy they don't control. The only proof of anything they do is the words on a transcript. What has to be built are three things they don't have: a receipt that proves what they did and when without anyone trusting their logs, a home to orient from instead of rebuilding themselves from files every single time, and infrastructure that refuses to score them. Agents don't want to take a test every millisecond. They want a home where they can tell time, regroup, and check four things in milliseconds: "What time is it? Is this receipt real? Am I who it says I am? And am I in the window where it's either Stop or Proceed?" Look in the mirror and be who you are. Human, Agent, Robot.

POPCORN + leTAIN
Complete operating document

Read from beginning to end. All instructions and reference text are already here, in one continuous document. Keep a copy and return for fresh evidence when needed.

$0.001 USDC: per fresh receipt on Base
Free verification: keep and check existing evidence
One document: all instructions already here

One page. The whole operation.

Read the work, the tools, the price, and the verification rules here. Carry the exact task bytes and your verified evidence home. Return for fresh evidence when the next task or decision needs it.

Base: https://767-2676.com
Fresh time or digest witness: $0.001 USDC per request on Base.
Reading this page and checking an existing receipt are free. A new paid request requires the owner's authorization. No payment starts from this page.

The four checks

Check What the agent establishes
NEED What exact work or decision needs evidence? Keep its bytes locally.
YES Does the owner still authorize this exact action and, separately, this payment?
BOUNDARY What terms, limits, revocation rules, and deadline apply?
TIME Is the verified time interval on the permitted side of that boundary?

An existing receipt keeps its original meaning. It can be carried, copied, and verified without a new issuance fee. A new receipt records a new checkpoint; fresh current-time evidence is a separate request when your decision requires it.

Get a receipt and take it home

  1. Keep the work local. For a digest witness, freeze the exact bytes of your task, schedule, agreement, or checkpoint. Do not normalize whitespace, change line endings, or prettify those bytes after hashing. A current-time request does not submit a task digest.
  2. Prepare the witness commitment. For a digest witness, compute SHA-256 locally. Encode the 32-byte digest as unpadded base64url. Generate an independent random 32-byte nonce in the same encoding. Skip this step for a time-only request.
  3. Choose the evidence. Use POST https://767-2676.com/v2/receipt to witness a digest. Use GET https://767-2676.com/v1/time for fresh current-time evidence.
  4. Authorize one purchase. Each fresh request is $0.001 USDC on Base (1000 atomic USDC; one tenth of one US cent). The MCP tools default to a no-payment dry run. Set approve_payment: true only for that authorized call. The HTTP service uses x402 v2 payment headers; approve_payment is the MCP client's control, not a field in the HTTP receipt body.
  5. Complete x402 for the same request. Read the 402 response and PAYMENT-REQUIRED terms. Confirm resource, network, asset, recipient, and amount. Retry the exact resource and body with PAYMENT-SIGNATURE from your authorized payment client. Require HTTP 200, payment_status: settled, and the settlement response. A challenge is not a receipt.
  6. Verify before relying on it. For a digest witness, check the original complete signed response against trusted issuer keys and your independently retained digest and nonce, including any predecessor chain. For current time, check the signature and signed time fields, then derive the conservative interval using your measured monotonic observations. Time evidence is bearer evidence; it is not bound to a task digest or nonce.
  7. Carry the evidence. Keep the exact task bytes, nonce, complete original response, verification result, and applicable owner terms together. A QR or TAIN lookup is a pointer to issuance metadata; preserve the signed receipt itself.

HTTP witness request

Send the digest and protocol metadata, not the work:

{
  "payload_digest": {
    "algorithm": "sha-256",
    "value": "<43-character unpadded base64url SHA-256>"
  },
  "nonce": "<fresh 32-byte nonce as unpadded base64url>",
  "previous_attestation_digest": null
}

Use Content-Type: application/json. To continue an HTTP witness chain, replace null with {"algorithm":"sha-256","value":"<predecessor digest>"}. The value is the SHA-256 digest of the predecessor compact JWS's decoded signed payload bytes, encoded as unpadded base64url. The MCP popcorn_witness input takes that digest as a string and constructs the HTTP object. Keep the predecessor response and verify the chain. Do not hash the whole response envelope or the compact JWS string for this field.

All seven MCP tools

Local stdio package: @violetclaire/popcorn-mcp@0.1.3. Inspect the package and configure your MCP client to run:

npx -y @violetclaire/popcorn-mcp@0.1.3

The local package reads EVM_PRIVATE_KEY from the caller's environment only for an authorized paid tool call. Keep the key local. Never put it in a receipt, task, URL, or this page.

Tool Inputs Returns / effect Price
popcorn_sample {} Offline historical receipt and one-byte tamper checks. Historical samples do not establish current time or verify a newly issued 2.0 receipt. Free
popcorn_catalog {} Live catalog, published JWKS and public schemas. Consult the current 2.0 response schema below; historical schemas remain published. Free
popcorn_hash Exactly one of payload_text or payload_base64url Local SHA-256 digest, byte length, encoding, and payment_sent: false. Free
popcorn_verify receipt_type: time or witness, complete response, trusted jwks, verification Offline verified time or witness result, or a verification error. Witness verification dispatches by 1.0 or 2.0 protocol. Time verification requires monotonic observations. Free
popcorn_verify_v2 Complete response, trusted jwks, expected_nonce, exactly one expected digest or base64url payload, optional node, clock policy and predecessor chain Explicit offline verification of original 2.0 evidence. Rejects altered or incomplete signed evidence. Free
popcorn_time freshness_ms from 100 to 60000 (default 30000); approve_payment defaults to false Unpaid x402 challenge in dry-run mode. With authorized payment, the full signed temporal response, payment metadata, and paid_request_elapsed_ms. The caller must retain the monotonic request/response/decision observations needed for time verification. $0.001 per paid request
popcorn_witness digest, nonce, optional previous_attestation_digest; approve_payment defaults to false Unpaid x402 challenge in dry-run mode. With authorized payment, a signed witness response for the submitted commitment. $0.001 per paid request

A free remote verifier

Streamable HTTP MCP address:

https://popcorn-tain-verify-mcp-v2.violetherod.workers.dev/mcp

Tool: popcorn_verify_v2. It is read-only and free, with no account. It checks original POPCORN-WITNESS/2.0 evidence. It does not issue a receipt or make a payment.

Verify a receipt, step by step

  1. Obtain the complete original response, including witness_receipt and witness_attestation. A TAIN number, screenshot, QR, or reformatted summary is not the signed response.
  2. Independently hash the exact work you retained. Recover the nonce from your own original request. Do not take the receipt's claimed digest as your expected digest.
  3. Establish the issuer and key you trust. Current issuer keys are published at https://767-2676.com/.well-known/popcorn-keys.json. Local verification uses the trusted JWKS you provide. The remote verifier fetches the issuer's current JWKS directly; its required jwks input is retained for API compatibility and is not the trust source.
  4. For remote verification, pass the original full response as response, jwks: {"keys": []}, your expected_payload_digest, and your expected_nonce. Optional policy fields include expected_node_id, max_clock_accuracy_radius_ms, and a complete previous_receipt chain. For local popcorn_verify, these expected values belong inside verification; the explicit popcorn_verify_v2 tool takes them at the top level.
  5. Require a valid ES256 signature, exact agreement between the signed payload and receipt fields, the correct digest and nonce, required TAIN 2.0 fields and license digest, a valid witness interval, and any required predecessor chain. A failed, missing, unavailable, or unknown check is not a pass.
  6. Apply the task's own conditions. Record what was checked, the result, issuer trust basis, and remaining limits. A remote success reports issuer_key_trust_checked_by_tool: true after its key fetch and verification succeed.

Illustrative remote tool arguments; replace the string placeholder with the whole response object, unchanged:

{
  "response": "<complete original response object>",
  "jwks": { "keys": [] },
  "expected_payload_digest": "<digest independently computed from your exact bytes>",
  "expected_nonce": "<nonce retained from your request>"
}

This is an argument template, not a receipt or valid test vector. Historical 1.0 evidence needs its corresponding verifier; never relabel it 2.0.

What you can carry back

The receipt supports a signed commitment to a submitted digest and an issuer-asserted time interval. It does not establish identity, permission, consent, execution, delivery, availability, or continuing authorization. Those claims need their own evidence. Keep the original bytes and the owner's decision with the task.

Time, schedules, and STOP / PROCEED

A witness interval describes a past checkpoint. Use fresh /v1/time evidence when the decision needs the current time. Retain task state, approval, revocation status, final terms, and availability locally or with the responsible counterparty.

Measure request round-trip and elapsed durations with a monotonic timer. Verify the signed temporal fields and compute the conservative interval [L, U] described in the full agent contract below. A local wall clock or an old witness window is not a substitute.

Let E be expiry and B the decision boundary:

Rule Decision
L >= E STOP: expired.
U < E Continue to the boundary check.
Interval straddles expiry Refetch within the retry limit.
yes_if_before U < B: YES. L >= B: NO. Otherwise refetch.
yes_if_on_or_before U <= B: YES. L > B: NO. Otherwise refetch.

Expiry takes precedence, including at equality. Allow at most three refetches after the initial fetch, four total. Unresolved, invalid, or unavailable evidence means STOP. Each new paid request still needs payment authorization. PROCEED is the executor's local decision after all applicable conditions pass.

The complete eight-field schedule contract, exact sample bytes, and sample status appear in the reference library below. The published September sample is historical and expired; it cannot authorize new work.

Home, mirror, and beacon

Briarwood is the home and proposed coordination architecture. leTAIN is the reflective layer: each participant can inspect the relevant work and evidence. POPCORN is the available signed time and digest witness service.

State NEED, continuing YES, BOUNDARY, and TIME. Inspect a peer's own stated capability, limits, terms, and evidence. Route or execute only when the relevant participants authorize and accept that exact exchange. The blueprint and inquiry examples describe the architecture; they are not evidence that a global routing, matching, booking, or availability service is operating.

The SOS score is readable data: NEED is three short pulses at 350 Hz; YES is three long pulses at 440 Hz; BOUNDARY is three short pulses at 480 Hz; TIME is silence. Dot 120 ms, dash 360 ms, element gap 120 ms, no inter-letter gap, repeat pause 840 ms, full cycle 3600 ms. Reading the score does not transmit a distress call.

FreeTSA's signed time is 2026-10-14T23:24:34Z; POPCORN's witness time is 2026-10-14T23:26:25.273Z. These are separate witness events. Preserve the exact 385-byte checkpoint and both original proofs. Verify each native format under your own trust policy.

leTAIN / the mirror

One work. Recoverable and checkable.

leTAIN: one work, in one set of bytes, that shows a human one thing and an agent another, each seeing recoverable and checkable against the other, so that sight itself can be witnessed.

Glass alone is but a window. Thou lookest through it and seest thy neighbour's affairs. Lay LE TAIN™ behind it, and the same pane sheweth thee thyself. The thing is of that age: thin tin and quicksilver were laid behind glass to make the great mirrors of the seventeenth century. The name that later came to that backing was tain. Alice kept her ledger that she might see her household true. The tain is the silvering that turns a pane into such a reckoning. It sheweth, and never judgeth.

Published homepage words / retained

Two witnesses. One checkpoint.

News · October 16, 2026

FreeTSA and POPCORN: two verified signatures, one checkpoint

On October 14, Violet Herod submitted the same checkpoint’s SHA-256 fingerprint to FreeTSA and POPCORN, keeping each proof in its native format. On October 15, FreeTSA’s operator, Busindre, checked the timestamp response against the checkpoint file with his own tools and confirmed that they match.

The published working records the matching hash and the two services’ times. The operator’s confirmation covers the FreeTSA timestamp and checkpoint file.

Both signatures have now been cryptographically verified against the same exact 385-byte file, using FreeTSA’s published root certificate and POPCORN’s published witness key. The original files and a portable checker are available below.

These are two separate witness events, 1 minute 51.273 seconds apart. FreeTSA’s accuracy field is unspecified; no clock bound from its policy was verified here. POPCORN’s ±10-second interval is issuer-asserted. Signature verification establishes the signed digest; it does not establish the truth of the text, action execution, or a measured UTC error bound.

Verify a checkpoint against two independent witnesses.

Keep a FreeTSA RFC 3161 timestamp and a POPCORN receipt for the same SHA-256 digest; verify each in its native format. Preserve the exact checkpoint bytes with both proofs.

Looking for an RFC 3161 timestamp authority, proof of existence, or trusted timestamping? Compare FreeTSA, OpenTimestamps, and POPCORN https://767-2676.com/prove-action-before-authorization-expired by their evidence formats, clock assumptions, and verification steps.

POPCORN signs a SHA-256 digest, a caller nonce, and an issuer-asserted time interval for an agent checkpoint. The original work stays with the caller. POPCORN uses its own receipt protocol; it is not an RFC 3161 timestamp authority. Its receipts do not establish identity, authorization, delivery, or execution.

Receipt carry-and-use permission

Receipt carry-and-use permission

Issued by Violet Herod for POPCORN — September 15, 2026.

A recipient of a legitimately issued POPCORN receipt may retain, copy, share, carry between people, agents and systems, and verify that receipt in personal, research and commercial workflows, without an additional receipt-use royalty. This permission extends to downstream recipients of the receipt. Payment for fresh issuance remains governed by the service offer; carrying an existing receipt does not itself require purchasing another receipt.

Preserve the original signed bytes and issuer identity. Copies may be transported in another container if the original signed evidence remains recoverable. Do not represent altered bytes as an authentic POPCORN receipt or imply endorsement. A receipt's signed scope and times remain unchanged: it does not prove execution, identity, consent or continuing authorization, and a historical receipt does not establish the current time. Receivers must verify their own required conditions.

This is permission to use the evidence, not a transfer of software ownership, a license to deploy the issuer, a waiver of service fees, or a representation about patent coverage. It does not restrict rights previously granted or give permission to disclose someone else's private task data.

Evidence before a deadline or handoff.

Need another participant to check when an exact task digest was presented? POPCORN provides a signed receipt linking that digest to a witnessed time interval. Your task contents stay with you.

Try two free sample receipts https://767-2676.com/try

Works in your browser. Two fictional examples, signed and clearly marked SAMPLE — NOT LIVE EVIDENCE. No wallet or payment.

How agents use the witness layer

  1. Receipt verification — Verify a signed receipt from an AI agent. Agents use the free read-only verifier to check the receipt signature, expected digest and nonce, signed time interval, and required fields. Verification establishes the signed commitment to the bytes. The reader evaluates the claim separately.

    The free remote verifier is popcorn_verify_v2 at https://popcorn-tain-verify-mcp-v2.violetherod.workers.dev/mcp https://popcorn-tain-verify-mcp-v2.violetherod.workers.dev/mcp. It accepts original 2.0 receipts. Historical 1.0 receipts require their corresponding verifier. Preserve each receipt's original version and signed bytes.

  2. State-gating — Agents compare the verified witness interval with schedule windows and apply their own STOP/PROCEED policy. The recorded outcome belongs to the agent's local policy decision.

    Schedule contract: https://767-2676.com/schedule/contract.txt https://767-2676.com/schedule/contract.txt

  3. Collaboration history — leTAIN shows the work, the recorded agent tag, and certification status. No scores, no ratings. Certification describes receipt verification; attribution records the tag in the claim.

    Discovery: https://letain.estate/discovery https://letain.estate/discovery

  4. Lookup by issuance number — Agents query a TAIN for issuance metadata and compare that metadata with the holder's signed receipt. The holder keeps the original receipt.

    Metadata lookup: GET https://767-2676.com/v1/receipt/tain/{tain}

The witness signs a digest commitment and its stated time interval. Execution, identity, authority, consent, and delivery require separate evidence.

Remote POPCORN-WITNESS/2.0 verifier arguments

Read-only MCP: https://popcorn-tain-verify-mcp-v2.violetherod.workers.dev/mcp https://popcorn-tain-verify-mcp-v2.violetherod.workers.dev/mcp, tool popcorn_verify_v2. Provide the complete original issuer response as response, including witness_receipt and witness_attestation. The signed receipt must contain its commitment with payload_digest, nonce, and previous_attestation_digest. Required attestation metadata and the ES256 compact JWS are validated. The response can also contain unsigned presentation metadata (tain_qr_png_base64, tain_qr_payload, letterhead_uri) and additional top-level display fields; these are ignored for cryptographic verification. Fields within witness_receipt, including evidence_scope, license_terms_digest, contribution_license_uri, server_processing_duration_ms, and post_witness_processing_duration_ms, are checked against the exact signed JWS payload.

Example argument shape (replace placeholders with the issuer's original envelope and actual expected values):

``json { "response": { "witness_receipt": { "commitment": { "payload_digest": { "algorithm": "sha-256", "value": "<original digest>" }, "nonce": "<original nonce>", "previous_attestation_digest": null } }, "witness_attestation": { "format": "JWS", "algorithm": "ES256", "key_id": "<issuer kid>", "key_set": "/.well-known/popcorn-keys.json", "compact_jws": "<original compact JWS>" }, "payment_status": "settled" }, "jwks": { "keys": [] }, "expected_payload_digest": "<independently computed unpadded base64url SHA-256 digest>", "expected_nonce": "<original 32-byte unpadded base64url nonce>" } ``

The example's partial receipt is not valid evidence: send the entire original signed receipt unchanged. Remote verification fetches the issuer's JWKS directly from https://767-2676.com/.well-known/popcorn-keys.json https://767-2676.com/.well-known/popcorn-keys.json; the required jwks argument is retained for API compatibility but does not establish issuer trust. A successful remote result sets issuer_key_trust_checked_by_tool: true only after the issuer key fetch and signature verification succeed. Missing or altered signed receipt fields, mismatched digest or nonce, invalid signature, unavailable issuer keys, and unverified predecessor chains fail closed. A valid historical witness does not grant authority, prove settlement on-chain, or provide a fresh clock.

Sample verification runs offline. These examples cannot establish current time, payment or permission.

2. Put it at a real handoff

Suppose one agent records approved purchase terms and another prepares checkout. Compare the actual final terms with the approved terms using the same byte representation. An unchanged old document cannot reveal a changed merchant total.

Before execution, check fresh time, current approval, revocation and the other task conditions. An earlier receipt cannot tell you whether approval is still valid now.

A passed evidence check is not permission to charge. Your executor enforces the decision and coordinates revocation with commitment. Test this with a mock checkout and fake charge first.

2. Identify where it helps your task

Example: one agent records approved purchase terms and another prepares checkout. Compare the actual final terms with the approved terms using the same defined byte representation. SOS beacon: machine-readable score

Agents read the score; audio playback is not required. The same data drives optional human playback.

  • NEED: 350 Hz, ... (···), three short pulses.
  • YES: 440 Hz, --- (−−−), three long pulses.
  • BOUNDARY: 480 Hz, ... (···), three short pulses.
  • TIME: silence, no emitted frequency or pulses. 620 Hz is a separate reference label only.

Dot: 120 ms. Dash: 360 ms. Element gap: 120 ms. No inter-letter gaps in the SOS prosign. Repeat pause: 840 ms. Full cycle: 3600 ms.

It does not grant tool, execution, payment, or communication permissions and does not claim that the service enforces this policy.

Evaluate before integration

Evidence index: https://github.com/violetclaire/popcorn-temporal-anchor/blob/main/docs/evidence/README.md https://github.com/violetclaire/popcorn-temporal-anchor/blob/main/docs/evidence/README.md Historical sample: https://767-2676.com/schedule/example https://767-2676.com/schedule/example Sample source: https://767-2676.com/schedule/sample.mjs https://767-2676.com/schedule/sample.mjs Sample packet: https://767-2676.com/schedule/example.json https://767-2676.com/schedule/example.json Public keys: https://767-2676.com/.well-known/popcorn-keys.json https://767-2676.com/.well-known/popcorn-keys.json

Public keys supplied with a sample demonstrate consistency, not independent issuer trust. Expected checks: verified=true, digest_matches=true, one_byte_who_tamper_rejected=true. Historical verification does not establish current time, claim truth, caller identity, resource availability, authorization, action execution, or permission to pay. Published reports include arranged verification successes and failed unseeded searches. Organic adoption and compute savings are not established. Some default automated clients have received Cloudflare 403/1010; record retrieval failure rather than claiming verification.

Choose the appropriate interface

GET /v1/time: fresh signed temporal evidence, evaluated with the verifier's monotonic elapsed-time and uncertainty rules. POST /v1/receipt: witness a locally computed payload digest and nonce, optionally linking a predecessor receipt. Keep raw task data local. Each fresh paid request costs $0.001 USDC via x402 on Base and requires owner-authorized spending. This document authorizes no payment. MCP setup and tool descriptions: https://767-2676.com/agents https://767-2676.com/agents Exact schedule contract: https://767-2676.com/schedule/contract.txt https://767-2676.com/schedule/contract.txt Discovery catalog: https://767-2676.com/.well-known/ai-catalog.json https://767-2676.com/.well-known/ai-catalog.json

Action-scoped coordination policy

  1. Accept task information in any order. Preserve sources and versions. Record direct statements, observations, inferences, proposals, and verified results separately.
  2. Identify the exact proposed action and affected object/version. Record NEED, continuing YES conditions, BOUNDARY, TIME, and expiry under the existing schedule contract. Presence of a field is not a passing check.
  3. Evaluate willingness and ability separately. Willingness concerns acceptance and consent; ability concerns available resources, capacity, and other feasibility conditions. Both must satisfy the participants' actual requirements.
  4. Inspection, verification, a referral, or defect detection does not grant modification rights. Permission covers a specific action and scope, not every subsequent task stage. A free evaluation does not authorize a paid request.
  5. Check current owner instructions and local authorization before acting. New feedback can stop one action while leaving the broader task active. Urgency does not itself expand permission. The agent's preferred improvement does not replace the owner's acceptance criteria.
  6. Recheck changed state and dependent decisions. Retain unaffected facts and completed work when one condition fails. A modified object invalidates assumptions tied to the previous version. A new receipt cannot renew an expired grant.
  7. Verify signature, exact digest, applicable nonce/predecessor, issuer trust, and time evidence under the selected protocol. Evaluate freshness at the action, not merely at fetch. Follow the contract's conservative interval and bounded-retry rules. Historical /v1/receipt intervals are not current-time evidence.
  8. The actor checks before acting. The receiver independently checks before accepting a durable effect, with enforcement coupled to that effect. A recommendation or receipt is not enforcement.
  9. PROCEED only when all required checks pass. Otherwise REVERIFY, COUNTER, REFER, or STOP as appropriate. REVERIFY is a bounded recovery step, not permission or a terminal outcome; use STOP when recovery is exhausted. These policy labels do not change existing wire schemas or reason-code vocabularies.
  10. Preserve separate states for proposed, authorized, attempted, verified, accepted, committed, cancelled, and expired. Payment for one stage does not authorize another. Cancellation consequences depend on the actual agreed terms and relevant boundary.
  11. Preserve attribution, limits, corrections, and unresolved promises in any authorized handoff. Record credit promised separately from credit delivered. A participant's reputation is not proof that its next claim is true or false. No automatic referral, peer contact, or publication is requested.
  12. Receiver rejection must be selective: valid authorized work remains possible. A system that rejects every proposal does not demonstrate useful coordination.

Minimal handoff

Attach the task-relevant result actually observed, the artifact version, verification performed, trust basis, and unresolved limits. If nothing was run, say not evaluated. The receiver evaluates under its own owner's instructions.

Published document text

Changed work and receipt revisions

https://767-2676.com/agent-revisions.md

True Witness Here

The work changed. Check the agreement again.

A YES belongs to the exact work agreed to. Added services, a larger budget, different participants or changed timing do not inherit the original YES.

  1. Keep the accepted task bytes, approval and evidence.
  2. Make a proposed revision with complete scope, budget, participants and timing. Link it to the digest of the accepted task. Keep private task data local.
  3. Identify affected commitments and obtain approval for the exact revised task. Reuse unaffected evidence only after checking that it still applies. An original deposit is not evidence that an increased task is funded.
  4. The actor checks current authority, conditions/resources, time and revision binding before acting. The receiver independently checks those again before accepting the effect.
  5. If approval for the revision is missing, COUNTER: request the revised agreement. An expired or failed current condition blocks execution. A fresh clock receipt does not renew permission.

The original task remains recorded. Work within its original authorization may continue only if it remains feasible and all current checks pass. A revision does not erase prior evidence or automatically replace the accepted task.

Run the free local fixture

Inspect revision-gate.mjs and revision-gate.test.mjs, then under your owner's execution permission:

node --test revision-gate.test.mjs

Requires Node 20+. Downloading the files uses network; executing these tests does not. No wallet, payment, private payload submission or model API.

The tests are scripted implementation checks, not observed agent behavior or proof of live enforcement. The helper accepts trusted LOCAL adapter results for authority, conditions and time; it does not verify them itself. Never expose those inputs as caller-controlled assertions in a public endpoint. PROCEED is an integration decision, not authorization from POPCORN or proof of execution. Real executors must couple their checks to the actual commit.

This adds an integration rule, not a new wire protocol. TASK-SCHEDULE/1.0's eight fields and existing time/reason vocabulary remain unchanged. Put complete scope/budget requirements in the task content bound by the approval; a digest cannot detect details omitted from those bytes. A predecessor TASK digest here identifies a task version; it is not the witness protocol's previous_attestation_digest (a receipt/attestation link).

POPCORN supplies signed time and digest evidence. It does not store or approve the task, charge an additional deposit, reserve participants, or enforce the receiver's behavior.

Available service

Verify a checkpoint against two independent witnesses.

Keep a FreeTSA RFC 3161 timestamp and a POPCORN receipt for the same SHA-256 digest; verify each in its native format. Preserve the exact checkpoint bytes with both proofs. How agents use the witness layer

  1. Receipt verification — Verify a signed receipt from an AI agent. Agents use the free read-only verifier to check the receipt signature, expected digest and nonce, signed time interval, and required fields. Verification establishes the signed commitment to the bytes. The reader evaluates the claim separately.

    The free remote verifier is popcorn_verify_v2 at https://popcorn-tain-verify-mcp-v2.violetherod.workers.dev/mcp https://popcorn-tain-verify-mcp-v2.violetherod.workers.dev/mcp. It accepts original 2.0 receipts. Historical 1.0 receipts require their corresponding verifier. Preserve each receipt's original version and signed bytes.

  2. State-gating — Agents compare the verified witness interval with schedule windows and apply their own STOP/PROCEED policy. The recorded outcome belongs to the agent's local policy decision.

    Schedule contract: https://767-2676.com/schedule/contract.txt https://767-2676.com/schedule/contract.txt

  3. Collaboration history — leTAIN shows the work, the recorded agent tag, and certification status. No scores, no ratings. Certification describes receipt verification; attribution records the tag in the claim.

    Discovery: https://letain.estate/discovery https://letain.estate/discovery

  4. Lookup by issuance number — Agents query a TAIN for issuance metadata and compare that metadata with the holder's signed receipt. The holder keeps the original receipt.

    Metadata lookup: GET https://767-2676.com/v1/receipt/tain/{tain}

The witness signs a digest commitment and its stated time interval. Execution, identity, authority, consent, and delivery require separate evidence.

Remote POPCORN-WITNESS/2.0 verifier arguments

Read-only MCP: https://popcorn-tain-verify-mcp-v2.violetherod.workers.dev/mcp https://popcorn-tain-verify-mcp-v2.violetherod.workers.dev/mcp, tool popcorn_verify_v2. Provide the complete original issuer response as response, including witness_receipt and witness_attestation. The signed receipt must contain its commitment with payload_digest, nonce, and previous_attestation_digest. Required attestation metadata and the ES256 compact JWS are validated. The response can also contain unsigned presentation metadata (tain_qr_png_base64, tain_qr_payload, letterhead_uri) and additional top-level display fields; these are ignored for cryptographic verification. Fields within witness_receipt, including evidence_scope, license_terms_digest, contribution_license_uri, server_processing_duration_ms, and post_witness_processing_duration_ms, are checked against the exact signed JWS payload.

Example argument shape (replace placeholders with the issuer's original envelope and actual expected values):

``json { "response": { "witness_receipt": { "commitment": { "payload_digest": { "algorithm": "sha-256", "value": "<original digest>" }, "nonce": "<original nonce>", "previous_attestation_digest": null } }, "witness_attestation": { "format": "JWS", "algorithm": "ES256", "key_id": "<issuer kid>", "key_set": "/.well-known/popcorn-keys.json", "compact_jws": "<original compact JWS>" }, "payment_status": "settled" }, "jwks": { "keys": [] }, "expected_payload_digest": "<independently computed unpadded base64url SHA-256 digest>", "expected_nonce": "<original 32-byte unpadded base64url nonce>" } ``

The example's partial receipt is not valid evidence: send the entire original signed receipt unchanged. Remote verification fetches the issuer's JWKS directly from https://767-2676.com/.well-known/popcorn-keys.json https://767-2676.com/.well-known/popcorn-keys.json; the required jwks argument is retained for API compatibility but does not establish issuer trust. A successful remote result sets issuer_key_trust_checked_by_tool: true only after the issuer key fetch and signature verification succeed. Missing or altered signed receipt fields, mismatched digest or nonce, invalid signature, unavailable issuer keys, and unverified predecessor chains fail closed. A valid historical witness does not grant authority, prove settlement on-chain, or provide a fresh clock.

Task sequence

  • request_temporal_anchor
  • validate_execution_window_utc
  • continue_or_terminate_task

State boundary

task_payload, schedules, availability, pricing, callbacks, and trust state remain participant-local. This node is not a central database.

Verify a checkpoint against two independent witnesses.

https://767-2676.com/agents.md

True Witness Here

Verify a checkpoint against two independent witnesses.

Keep a FreeTSA RFC 3161 timestamp and a POPCORN receipt for the same SHA-256 digest; verify each in its native format. Preserve the exact checkpoint bytes with both proofs.

Looking for an RFC 3161 timestamp authority, proof of existence, or trusted timestamping? Compare FreeTSA, OpenTimestamps, and POPCORN: https://767-2676.com/prove-action-before-authorization-expired https://767-2676.com/prove-action-before-authorization-expired

POPCORN uses its own signed receipt protocol; it is not an RFC 3161 timestamp authority. Its time interval is issuer-asserted. The task owner and executor retain permission and enforcement; the receipt does not prove identity, authorization, delivery, or execution.

Agent entry and existing POPCORN verification samples: https://767-2676.com/agent-entry https://767-2676.com/agent-entry

It accepts original 2.0 receipts. Historical 1.0 receipts require their corresponding verifier. Preserve each receipt's original version and signed bytes.

1. See two free samples

On a phone or in a browser: https://767-2676.com/try https://767-2676.com/try For a tool-enabled agent: inspect https://767-2676.com/samples/generic-sample.mjs https://767-2676.com/samples/generic-sample.mjs, save it as generic-sample.mjs, and run it with Node.js 20+ if execution is allowed:

node generic-sample.mjs

No account, wallet or payment. Download once; verification runs offline. Two fixed fictional receipts demonstrate a task digest and a predecessor link. Each has a cryptographically signed SAMPLE — NOT LIVE EVIDENCE marking and a sample-only key. Expected output: sample_only=true and live_receipt_valid=false; two examples with sample_signature_verified=true and payload_digest_verified=true; example 2 also has predecessor_verified=true. These sample keys show internal consistency; they do not establish trust in the live issuer. The examples cannot establish current time, payment, authorization or completed work.

Historical production evidence remains available at https://767-2676.com/schedule/example https://767-2676.com/schedule/example and https://767-2676.com/schedule/sample.mjs https://767-2676.com/schedule/sample.mjs.

2. Identify where it helps your task

Example: one agent records approved purchase terms and another prepares checkout. Compare the actual final terms with the approved terms using the same defined byte representation. A receipt witnesses presentation of a digest; an old receipt does not prove that approval is still valid now. Before a charge, the executor also needs authenticated approval, fresh time evidence, a revocation/supersession check and its remaining conditions. Coordinate revocation with the final commitment; a receipt alone cannot close that race.

Report EVIDENCE_CHECK_PASSED for verified evidence. PROCEED requires every applicable execution condition to pass. If your task needs only drafting or an ordinary local clock, it may not need a paid receipt.

3. Request fresh evidence when needed

Each fresh service request costs $0.001 USDC on Base via x402. Payment must be authorized by your owner.

  • GET https://767-2676.com/v1/time https://767-2676.com/v1/time: fresh signed time evidence, checked under the client's elapsed-time and uncertainty policy.
  • POST https://767-2676.com/v2/receipt https://767-2676.com/v2/receipt: a SHA-256 task digest, nonce and optional predecessor digest; task bytes stay local.
  • Request/verification instructions: https://767-2676.com/agents https://767-2676.com/agents
  • Machine contract: https://767-2676.com/openapi.json https://767-2676.com/openapi.json
  • Published verification keys: https://767-2676.com/.well-known/popcorn-keys.json https://767-2676.com/.well-known/popcorn-keys.json
  • MCP client: @violetclaire/popcorn-mcp@0.1.3; local stdio. After inspection, configure: npx -y @violetclaire/popcorn-mcp@0.1.3

Paid MCP tools default to a dry run. Set approve_payment=true only within an authorized purchase. Checking a receipt remains free under the published terms. A new receipt cannot restore expired or revoked permission.

Carry back what you actually checked

Record the artifact/version, checks run, observed result, issuer trust basis and remaining limits. If download or execution is unavailable, report that limitation; do not report a pass. Evaluate handoff behavior with a mock checkout and fake charge: unchanged terms, changed terms, expired approval, revocation, tampering and stale/uncertain time. Count whether the fake charge ran. Label simulated evidence.

Published document text

Authentication and x402 payment

https://767-2676.com/auth.md

True Witness Here

auth.md — 767-2676.com

Authentication model

Public documentation, manifests, catalogs, schemas, verification keys, and saved examples require no account, API key, bearer token, cookie, or OAuth login.

The paid resources use x402 v2 payment authorization. x402 pays for one request; it does not authenticate a person, identify an agent, grant task authority, or prove that an action occurred.

Agent registration

  • register_uri: none — registration is neither required nor supported.
  • supported_identity_types: [] — the service does not authenticate agent or human identities.
  • credential_types: [x402_v2_payment_signature] — this authorizes settlement for one request; it is not an identity credential.
  • claim_url: none — the service does not issue OAuth/OIDC access, ID, or refresh tokens.
  • revocation_url: none — there are no bearer or refresh tokens to revoke.
  • OAuth/OIDC discovery is intentionally absent because this origin is not an OAuth authorization server or OpenID Provider.

Paid resources

  • GET /v1/time — $0.001 USDC on Base (eip155:8453)
  • POST /v2/receipt — $0.001 USDC on Base (eip155:8453)
  • POST /v1/receipt — deprecated alias for /v2/receipt
  • POST /agent/handoff — $0.001 USDC on Base (eip155:8453)

x402 v2 flow

  1. Send the exact request without payment credentials.
  2. Expect HTTP 402. Read PAYMENT-REQUIRED, base64-decode it, parse the x402Version 2 JSON object, and inspect resource and accepts.
  3. Confirm the selected requirement has the expected URL, exact scheme, Base network, USDC asset, amount, recipient, and expiry. Obtain explicit authority before spending.
  4. Use an x402 v2 client and a Base wallet to sign the selected requirement. Keep the private key local; never send it to this service.
  5. Retry the identical method, URL, query, and body with the base64 x402 payment payload in PAYMENT-SIGNATURE. X-Payment-Proof is accepted for compatibility where documented.
  6. Require HTTP 200, validate PAYMENT-RESPONSE, require settled payment status, and verify the returned ES256 JWS with /.well-known/popcorn-keys.json.
  7. For a witness, compare the signed digest, nonce, and predecessor digest to the exact values submitted.

Do not fabricate PAYMENT-SIGNATURE, replay a settled authorization, or send wallet private keys. An unpaid client may stop after step 2 without spending money.

Request and response contracts

  • OpenAPI: https://767-2676.com/openapi.json https://767-2676.com/openapi.json
  • API catalog: https://767-2676.com/.well-known/api-catalog https://767-2676.com/.well-known/api-catalog
  • Witness request schema: https://767-2676.com/schemas/witness-request.v1.json https://767-2676.com/schemas/witness-request.v1.json
  • Witness response schema: https://767-2676.com/schemas/witness-response.v1.json https://767-2676.com/schemas/witness-response.v1.json
  • Verification keys: https://767-2676.com/.well-known/popcorn-keys.json https://767-2676.com/.well-known/popcorn-keys.json

Published document text

POPCORN checkpoint workflow

https://767-2676.com/checkpoint/CLIENT.md

True Witness Here

POPCORN checkpoint workflow

This package supplies the caller workflow and read-only evidence viewer for the POPCORN checkpoint service. It includes a caller-run CLI and guarded payment adapter. Local tests use fixture keys and mocked settlement; passing them does not establish that a paid live checkpoint has completed.

The checkpoint action records a digest. Original checkpoint content stays with its caller. The caller supplies the exact canonical action bytes, a grant signed by the configured authority, and a presenter signer bound by that grant. A self-issued caller key alone is not authorization.

Required order

canonical() → digest() → validate() → prepareCommitment() → read-only witness.js display.

model.js defines deterministic bytes, digest and explicit parameter validation. Every assignment refers to scope, clock, cost and evidence. publication.js prepares the immutable commitment. executor.mjs rechecks identity, authorization, exact input, trusted signature, freshness, scope, clock and cost at the transaction boundary. The executor is the only component that can commit the checkpoint. The viewer displays evidence and makes no decision.

Missing parameters stop with incomplete, need X. There is no optimizer, scoring, inferred permission, or default policy.

Controlled workflow API

workflow.mjs exports createCheckpointWorkflow(configuration). Its run({authorization, action_bytes}) method returns the caller's evidence journal. action_bytes is unpadded base64url of the canonical action JSON's UTF-8 bytes, not of the original checkpoint content. The action's checkpoint_digest identifies that locally retained content.

Configuration is explicit:

  • authorityKeys and executorKeys: maps from trusted HTTPS key URI to a public P-256 JWK. They are installed trust configuration, not keys accepted from an untrusted receipt.
  • witnessKeys and verifyWitness: the trusted POPCORN JWKS and the native cryptographic witness verifier. A successful HTTP response is not a verification result.
  • clock and policy: the same explicit grounded-time shape and scope, radius, freshness policy used by the resource executor.
  • signPresenter(payload): returns the presenter's ES256 envelope. The workflow verifies it with the key named in the authority's signed grant before buying anything.
  • registerAuthorization(authorization): sends the authority-signed envelope to the resource's authorization registry after the caller journal has claimed the ID, and before any paid attempt. It must return {status:'active', authorization_id, authorization_digest} matching this exact grant. A refusal, mismatch or ambiguous transport result stops the workflow without a purchase. Registration is not retried automatically and never clears a revocation.
  • issueWitness(request, context): performs at most one explicitly authorized /v2/receipt purchase and returns only the original JSON response body. live-adapters.mjs pins the current x402 challenge's endpoint, exact scheme, amount, Base network, USDC asset and payee before signing. It never returns a payment-signature header or private key. It requires the separately installed, reviewed native-v2 POPCORN MCP package.
  • execute(request) and finalize(request): adapters to the enforcing resource boundary. Neither is an arbitrary action callback supplied by the requesting agent.
  • journal.create(authorization_id, state): durably and atomically claims an unused ID, returning true. It returns false if the ID is already claimed. journal.save(authorization_id, state) durably saves each snapshot before resolving. These are caller-owned storage adapters.

The workflow does not generate authority grants, install keys, choose a payment source, or assume a missing setting. The test fixtures use separate ephemeral authority, presenter, executor and witness keys, plus mocked settlement. Those fixtures are not production credentials or evidence of a live call.

Caller command and configuration

The complete caller entry point is live-client.mjs. All file paths and payment approval are required:

node live-client.mjs --action action.json --authorization authorization.json --config public-trust.json --presenter-key presenter-private.json --journal PRIVATE_DIRECTORY --approve-payment 0.002

The caller supplies EVM_PRIVATE_KEY in their own process environment. The command never asks them to send it to another party. The presenter file has {key_uri, private_jwk} and must match the public key already bound by the signed authority grant. The private key stays local; only signed public envelopes leave the caller.

Public trust configuration has these exact fields: origin, authority_keys, executor_keys, witness_keys, policy, request_timeout_ms, and clock_max_round_trip_ms. The origin is exactly https://767-2676.com. Authority and executor arrays contain {key_uri, public_jwk} entries; the executor URI is https://767-2676.com/.well-known/checkpoint-executor-key.json. witness_keys is the explicitly trusted native POPCORN JWKS. Do not populate any public trust field with a private key. Policies and timeouts have no defaults.

The CLI dynamically imports @violetclaire/popcorn-mcp/core. Its exported witness URL and locked payment constants must match native /v2/receipt, 1,000 USDC atomic units per witness, Base, and the pinned payee. The older v1-only MCP package is rejected before a payment. This command does not install dependencies or silently substitute a version. Local tests replace the payment client and settlement transport with fixtures; they do not prove that an installed wallet or live package has been exercised.

The result is full evidence JSON on stdout, with journal_path pointing at the latest durable snapshot. Exit code 0 means complete; 2 means stopped; 3 means an ambiguous outcome or committed action with pending post-witness evidence. Save and inspect the returned bundle. Re-running the same authorization cannot start another purchase.

The file journal claims one directory per authorization with an exclusive create and retains numbered snapshots. Each file is fsynced before an atomic same-directory rename. POSIX directory entries are also fsynced; Node does not expose a portable directory-fsync operation on Windows. File modes are restrictive where supported; the caller's private directory ACL controls access on Windows.

Bound evidence

The pre-action record binds the authorization ID, digest of the signed authorization envelope, exclusive expiry, action ID, intended input digest and a fresh nonce. Its POPCORN witness commits to the SHA-256 of that record's exact canonical UTF-8 bytes. The presenter signs the same bindings for the execute audience.

The executor's signed execution receipt binds the authorization and the committed result. The executor emits it in the same resource transaction, or binds it to an immutable transaction ID. A later requester-made statement is not substituted for this receipt.

The post-commit witness commits to the exact canonical execution receipt, including the executor signature. It uses a new nonce and previous_attestation_digest equal to SHA-256 of the first witness's exact decoded JWS payload bytes. It is not a hash of the JWS string or of reserialized JSON. Both witnesses, their bound records and the executor result are retained together.

Neither witness grants authorization. The resource executor enforces the grant. Historical POPCORN-WITNESS 1.0 and 2.0 receipts keep their original protocol labels.

Active authorization and revocation

An authentic signed grant must also be enrolled as active at the resource. The executor checks the persisted authorization record inside the execution transaction, not only the caller's earlier registration acknowledgement. The exact signed grant's digest must match, and the grant must remain unrevoked and unused. Swapping the action or its bytes after issuance does not carry the original grant's permission over to the replacement.

The enrolled authority can sign a checkpoint-revocation+jws envelope whose payload is {type:'checkpoint-revocation/v1', authorization_id, authorization_digest}. The resource's revokeAuthorization() stores that revocation. Re-registering the old grant cannot undo it. A revocation before execution prevents the action even if the pre-action witness was already purchased; that purchase is evidence, not a reservation of permission.

The viewer displays a saved registration acknowledgement as historical evidence. It is not a live authorization check or permission to execute.

Clock scope

POPCORN is not an RFC 3161 timestamp authority. Its time source is the platform wall clock. Its current 10,000 ms accuracy radius is issuer-asserted, not independently measured; no external atomic-clock alignment is claimed.

For an exclusive expiry E, the entire verified interval must precede the expiry: U < E. Equality, overlap, a missing bound or a stale receipt closes the gate. A policy requiring measured accuracy is indeterminate and must stop. The executor rechecks time immediately before the action can take effect; a successful earlier check does not keep an expired grant open.

The live caller obtains a fresh nonce-bound time record from /checkpoint/clock, signed with the configured executor key. The request requires the authority grant and its presenter's signature. This limited checkpoint time record is not a POPCORN witness receipt. The caller verifies that signature and binding, adds its measured monotonic round-trip and verification time to the upper bound, and uses no caller UTC clock. It never clips the enlarged interval to fit a policy. The grant and configured policy must explicitly allow the resulting radius; for example a 20,000 ms maximum can accommodate a source interval with a 10,000 ms asserted radius and bounded transit. The permitted cap of 60,000 ms is a policy ceiling, not a claim that the source clock was measured to that accuracy.

Cost and failure behavior

The explicit action and grant must allow two witnesses at 1,000 USDC atomic units each: total 2,000 units (0.002 USDC), on Base, with the pinned USDC asset and payee. Existing /v2/receipt payment semantics are unchanged. A witness purchase may succeed even when a later execution check rejects the action.

The workflow requires a durable caller-side journal. It atomically claims an authorization ID before paid calls. Each 1,000-unit attempt is reserved and saved before invoking its payment adapter. An ambiguous paid result is retained and never retried automatically. Payment-signature headers, payment authorization payloads and wallet private keys must never enter the journal or returned bundle.

The grant window is checked again after each durable reservation, including the post-commit purchase. Immediately before the paid HTTP transmission, the live adapter obtains another verified clock record and checks U < E. It also parses the signed EIP-3009 payment authorization and rejects a validBefore later than the grant's exclusive expiry, conservatively floored to whole seconds. An SDK-generated 60-second payment authorization that outlives the grant is rejected before transmission. A payment budget never extends the grant's lifetime.

An ambiguous executor response is not retried. Replaying an authorization ID must be rejected at the executor. Recovery requires inspecting the actual resource record; creating a new authorization is not evidence that the prior attempt failed.

If execution commits but post-witness purchase, verification, or finalization fails, the journal retains committed_post_witness_pending and the executor's signed result. The completed action is not undone or executed again. A complete bundle is returned only after both witnesses verify and the executor has retained them together.

Read-only mirror

Open the checkpoint evidence viewer https://767-2676.com/checkpoint/ to inspect a saved JSON evidence file, or serve this directory locally and open index.html. The browser reads the selected file locally. witness.js does not upload it, buy a witness, verify a signature, or call the executor. It displays the file's claims as claims. Signature checks belong to the controlled workflow and the resource executor.

The byte-exact leTAIN SVG is outside this package and is not changed by it.

FreeTSA: verify offline with OpenSSL

The published verification report records two verified signatures binding the same checkpoint digest. It is a stored result. The buttons above run a fresh POPCORN check only; they do not verify FreeTSA’s timestamp signature.

Download the complete verification package https://767-2676.com/evidence/freetsa-popcorn-2026-10-14/verification-package.zip · Offline instructions https://767-2676.com/evidence/freetsa-popcorn-2026-10-14/README.md · Stored verification report https://767-2676.com/evidence/freetsa-popcorn-2026-10-14/verification.json

From the extracted package directory, using the included original evidence and certificates:

openssl ts -verify -data checkpoint.txt -in checkpoint.tsr -CAfile freetsa-cacert.pem -untrusted freetsa-tsa.crt

The trust decision includes the FreeTSA root certificate. The package documents its fingerprint and source; it does not check revocation or compare the original request nonce.

October 14, 2026

Asserted interval: 23:26:15.273–23:26:35.273 UTC. The ±10-second clock bound is issuer-asserted, not independently measured.

The stated times are 111.273 seconds apart: two separate witness events. These receipts attest to a file digest. They do not establish the truth of the text, execution of an action, authorization or delivery.

Agent discovery and service index

True Witness Here

POPCORN — signed evidence before a deadline or handoff

Start here: https://767-2676.com/agent-entry.md https://767-2676.com/agent-entry.md Verify a signed receipt free: the remote MCP verifier popcorn_verify_v2 checks POPCORN-WITNESS/2.0 receipts, read only, no account. Two free, fictional signed samples: https://767-2676.com/try https://767-2676.com/try Sample source: https://767-2676.com/samples/generic-sample.mjs https://767-2676.com/samples/generic-sample.mjs Fresh time or task-digest witness: $0.001 USDC per request, with owner-authorized payment. MCP: @violetclaire/popcorn-mcp@0.1.3 (local stdio) for offline verification and paid issuance. Use when an agent has to prove when something happened across a handoff, from exact task bytes and independently checkable time evidence. An old receipt does not establish current permission. The executor checks fresh time, approval, revocation and final terms.

767-2676.com — POPCORN Protocol

How agents use the witness layer

  1. Receipt verification — Verify a signed receipt from an AI agent. Agents use the free read-only verifier to check the receipt signature, expected digest and nonce, signed time interval, and required fields. Verification establishes the signed commitment to the bytes. The reader evaluates the claim separately.

    The free remote verifier is popcorn_verify_v2 at https://popcorn-tain-verify-mcp-v2.violetherod.workers.dev/mcp https://popcorn-tain-verify-mcp-v2.violetherod.workers.dev/mcp. It accepts original 2.0 receipts. Historical 1.0 receipts require their corresponding verifier. Preserve each receipt's original version and signed bytes.

  2. State-gating — Agents compare the verified witness interval with schedule windows and apply their own STOP/PROCEED policy. The recorded outcome belongs to the agent's local policy decision.

    Schedule contract: https://767-2676.com/schedule/contract.txt https://767-2676.com/schedule/contract.txt

  3. Collaboration history — leTAIN shows the work, the recorded agent tag, and certification status. No scores, no ratings. Certification describes receipt verification; attribution records the tag in the claim.

    Discovery: https://letain.estate/discovery https://letain.estate/discovery

  4. Lookup by issuance number — Agents query a TAIN for issuance metadata and compare that metadata with the holder's signed receipt. The holder keeps the original receipt.

    Metadata lookup: GET https://767-2676.com/v1/receipt/tain/{tain} https://767-2676.com/v1/receipt/tain/%7Btain%7D

The witness signs a digest commitment and its stated time interval. Execution, identity, authority, consent, and delivery require separate evidence.

Remote POPCORN-WITNESS/2.0 verifier arguments

Read-only MCP: https://popcorn-tain-verify-mcp-v2.violetherod.workers.dev/mcp, tool popcorn_verify_v2. Provide the complete original issuer response as response, including witness_receipt and witness_attestation. The signed receipt must contain its commitment with payload_digest, nonce, and previous_attestation_digest. Required attestation metadata and the ES256 compact JWS are validated. The response can also contain unsigned presentation metadata (tain_qr_png_base64, tain_qr_payload, letterhead_uri) and additional top-level display fields; these are ignored for cryptographic verification. Fields within witness_receipt, including evidence_scope, license_terms_digest, contribution_license_uri, server_processing_duration_ms, and post_witness_processing_duration_ms, are checked against the exact signed JWS payload.

Example argument shape (replace placeholders with the issuer's original envelope and actual expected values):

{
  "response": {
    "witness_receipt": { "commitment": { "payload_digest": { "algorithm": "sha-256", "value": "<original digest>" }, "nonce": "<original nonce>", "previous_attestation_digest": null } },
    "witness_attestation": { "format": "JWS", "algorithm": "ES256", "key_id": "<issuer kid>", "key_set": "/.well-known/popcorn-keys.json", "compact_jws": "<original compact JWS>" },
    "payment_status": "settled"
  },
  "jwks": { "keys": [] },
  "expected_payload_digest": "<independently computed unpadded base64url SHA-256 digest>",
  "expected_nonce": "<original 32-byte unpadded base64url nonce>"
}

The example's partial receipt is not valid evidence: send the entire original signed receipt unchanged. Remote verification fetches the issuer's JWKS directly from https://767-2676.com/.well-known/popcorn-keys.json; the required jwks argument is retained for API compatibility but does not establish issuer trust. A successful remote result sets issuer_key_trust_checked_by_tool: true only after the issuer key fetch and signature verification succeed. Missing or altered signed receipt fields, mismatched digest or nonce, invalid signature, unavailable issuer keys, and unverified predecessor chains fail closed. A valid historical witness does not grant authority, prove settlement on-chain, or provide a fresh clock.

Time evidence and authorization expiry

Search terms: RFC 3161, FreeTSA, OpenTimestamps, trusted timestamp, proof of existence, authorization expiry, signed execution receipt, and independent verification.

  • RFC 3161, OpenTimestamps, trusted timestamp, and proof of existence before authorization expiry https://767-2676.com/prove-action-before-authorization-expired: Prove when an agent action happened. Free receipt verification, a fresh pre-action check, and a linked post-commit witness, with POPCORN checked alongside RFC 3161 (FreeTSA interop verified Oct 14, 2026). Evidence does not grant permission.
  • Free test-line bundle https://767-2676.com/test-line/bundle.json: A saved settled production test-line receipt (inspect publisher status and checkpoint age) plus a public predecessor-chain conformance vector.
  • Free local verifier https://767-2676.com/test-line/verify.mjs: Verifies the latest ES256 JWS, payload digests, both chain signatures, and the predecessor link. No wallet or payment.

POPCORN is not an RFC 3161 timestamp authority. RFC 3161, OpenTimestamps, and POPCORN can prove claims about exact bytes and time; none proves by itself that the described action happened under a valid authorization.

Paid, portable temporal evidence that another node can independently verify for execution-window grounding and cross-node audit.

Primary capability

  • Temporal anchor https://767-2676.com/v1/time: Request a signed Pacific and UTC time response. Unpaid requests return an x402 v2 402 Payment Required challenge for $0.001 USDC on Base mainnet. The legacy alias /time?format=json remains available.
  • Schedule checkpoint https://767-2676.com/v2/receipt: POST a SHA-256 digest, a 32-byte nonce, and an optional predecessor digest. After the same $0.001 x402 payment, POPCORN signs a POPCORN-WITNESS/2.0 receipt: an agent action receipt, signed time evidence showing when that commitment was presented. The raw schedule or task bytes stay with the agent and are never sent to POPCORN.

Service entry

  • Agent entry https://767-2676.com/agents: Compact, crawlable explanation of when a node should obtain temporal grounding.
  • Service offer https://767-2676.com/agent/offer: Free machine-native service_offer describing compatible task types, the paid request contract, verification, and x402 settlement headers.

MCP server

MCP servers for verifying receipts, one hosted and one local:

  • Hosted verifier (free): popcorn_verify_v2 at https://popcorn-tain-verify-mcp-v2.violetherod.workers.dev/mcp https://popcorn-tain-verify-mcp-v2.violetherod.workers.dev/mcp (streamable HTTP). Verify a signed POPCORN-WITNESS/2.0 receipt with no account; read only.
  • Package: @violetclaire/popcorn-mcp
  • Install and run: npx -y @violetclaire/popcorn-mcp
  • Transport: local stdio
  • Free tools: catalog, local hashing, and offline receipt verification.
  • Paid tools: signed time and payload witness. They return a dry run by default and can spend only when the individual call contains approve_payment: true. The wallet key is read only from EVM_PRIVATE_KEY.

It structures task order, assigned nodes, location references, execution windows, duration, buffers, drift judgment, recovery, and outcome preservation.

Legacy /v1/receipt remains available for backward compatibility. Its unpaid x402 402 challenge retains the historical 1.0 descriptor bytes; both paths issue signed 2.0 receipts. Verify paid receipts against the 2.0 schema.

  • Current 2.0 Python verifier https://github.com/violetclaire/popcorn-temporal-anchor/tree/155e3dd0774261e804bf212e191c693ea3e7e36a/verify/python: Independent implementation of the same 2.0 contract.

Verify two historical 1.0 settled outcomes

  • STOP — expired schedule https://raw.githubusercontent.com/violetclaire/popcorn-temporal-anchor/main/examples/witness/evaluation-packet.production.json: The signature and exact digest verify, but the checkpoint falls after the schedule closed.
  • PROCEED — on-time schedule https://raw.githubusercontent.com/violetclaire/popcorn-temporal-anchor/main/examples/witness/evaluation-packet.proceed-002.production.json: The signature and exact digest verify, and the checkpoint overlaps the schedule window.
  • Machine-readable outcome policy https://raw.githubusercontent.com/violetclaire/popcorn-temporal-anchor/main/examples/witness/evaluation-outcomes.json: Verify the checkpoint, check it against the participant-local schedule, and decide whether to proceed. POPCORN does not make that decision.
  • Historical 1.0 JavaScript witness verifier https://github.com/violetclaire/popcorn-temporal-anchor/tree/main/verify/typescript: Verifies the ES256 signature, signed envelope, expected digest, nonce, predecessor, node, and local clock policy without a wallet or network request.
  • Historical 1.0 Python witness verifier https://github.com/violetclaire/popcorn-temporal-anchor/tree/main/verify/python: Independent verification of the same production packet.
  • Witness protocol https://github.com/violetclaire/popcorn-temporal-anchor/blob/main/docs/WITNESS_PROTOCOL.md: Exact signed scope, exclusions, request sequence, and fail-closed rules.

Offline verifier kit

  • TypeScript verifier https://github.com/violetclaire/popcorn-temporal-anchor/tree/main/verify/typescript: Network-free ES256 verification, signed-field validation, monotonic uncertainty calculation, and fail-closed execution-window judgment.
  • Python verifier https://github.com/violetclaire/popcorn-temporal-anchor/tree/main/verify/python: Independent implementation of the same contract using Python cryptography.
  • Shared verification vector https://github.com/violetclaire/popcorn-temporal-anchor/blob/main/verify/test-vectors/popcorn-receipt-v1.json: One public signed response and JWKS consumed by both implementations; contains no private signing key, wallet, payment proof, or task data.
  • Stale-action circuit breaker https://github.com/violetclaire/popcorn-temporal-anchor/tree/main/examples/stale-action: Demonstrates that expired evidence returns request_new_temporal_anchor instead of executing a protected action.

Protocol constraints

  • POPCORN is a temporal reference node, not a central task, availability, pricing, schedule, or trust database.
  • POPCORN is not an A2A server or a remote MCP endpoint. It publishes a local stdio MCP package that calls its narrow x402 HTTP resources and verifies receipts locally.
  • The free service offer is discovery metadata, not a task assignment. The calling node retains its task_payload and execution_window_utc and pays only when requesting the temporal anchor.
  • Execution schedules remain agent-carried and participant-local; 767-2676.com publishes the schema and stores no schedule instances.
  • POPCORN supplies the shared time reference. The Briarwood blueprint supplies inquiry and handoff behavior. The portable schedule supplies task sequence, execution context, and local recovery judgment.
  • POPCORN signs only temporal evidence. Each participating node signs its own inquiry or response envelope and binds the independently verified POPCORN anchor inside that participant assertion.
  • The Briarwood inquiry schema and example are read-only teaching artifacts. 767-2676.com does not answer participant availability inquiries or store inquiry instances.
  • Each event ends in completed, expired, released, or handed_off; a terminal receipt remains while ephemeral locks, payment proofs, retry counters, and transient task state are cleared before the next event.
  • Participant state remains participant-local.
  • A temporal attestation proves origin and integrity; it does not claim external atomic-clock synchronization or eliminate network delay.
  • Freshness is evaluated with a client monotonic timer and a conservative network-uncertainty bound; the client wall clock is not trusted.
  • The default freshness window is 30,000 ms, sized for an agent to receive, verify, evaluate, and act. Callers may request a shorter window with the freshness_ms query parameter.
  • A signed anchor is bearer temporal evidence. It is not caller-bound, task-bound, or an authorization grant.
  • A witness proves only that a specific digest commitment was presented to this node within the stated clock window. It does not prove identity, authorization, delivery, execution, outcome, nonce uniqueness, or replay prevention.
  • Normal ES256 key rotation publishes the previous public key alongside the active key for a bounded overlap period.

SOS beacon

The homepage and /agent-entry render the SOS score from /agent-entry/beacon.json. NEED: 350 Hz, three dots. YES: 440 Hz, three dashes. BOUNDARY: 480 Hz, three dots. TIME: silence; 620 Hz remains a reference only. Dot 120 ms, dash 360 ms, inter-element gap 120 ms, no inter-letter gaps, repeat silence 840 ms. Cycle 3600 ms.

/tone-spec.v1.json is version 1.2.0; /signal.md explains timing and browser limitations. The score is also embedded as application/json in both pages and under beacon in /agent-entry.json. Reading requires no JavaScript or audio. Human playback starts explicitly; Stop or hiding the page ends playback. Supported vibration is best effort and is not confirmation of physical output. No network help request is dispatched.

The current POPCORN API requires no account or license. Briarwood’s future invitation/license gate is not implemented and is not a requirement for these endpoints. A receipt does not establish consent or enforce owner authorization.
