Authorization expiry and independent time evidence

https://767-2676.com/prove-action-before-authorization-expired

True Witness Here
767-2676.com https://767-2676.com/ · agents https://767-2676.com/agents · llms.txt https://767-2676.com/llms.txt

Proving an action happened before an authorization expired: RFC 3161, OpenTimestamps, and a fresh witness

Fetch a public receipt chain and verify it locally. This costs nothing, creates no account, and sends no private data:

curl --fail --silent --show-error https://767-2676.com/test-line/bundle.json --output popcorn-test-line-bundle.json
curl --fail --silent --show-error https://767-2676.com/test-line/verify.mjs --output verify-popcorn-chain.mjs
node verify-popcorn-chain.mjs popcorn-test-line-bundle.json

Expected result:

latest_signature=valid
latest_payload_digest=valid
predecessor_signature=valid
current_signature=valid
payload_digests=valid
chain_link=valid
authorization_granted=false
action_execution_proven=false

The last two lines are the point. A trusted timestamp or proof of existence can establish that exact bytes existed before a time. It does not prove that the action described by those bytes happened, or that its authorization was valid. Authorization expiry requires an explicit binding between the permission, action, and evidence.

POPCORN packages a caller-chosen nonce, an explicit predecessor link, and an asserted time interval into a fresh ES256 receipt for 0.001 USDC on Base, verifiable offline for pre-action checks and post-commit audit.

The bundle reports the publisher status and checkpoint time. Hourly tests are a target cadence, not a freshness guarantee. Historical payload text is preserved for digest verification; a saved sample is not a fresh pre-action witness. The public chain is a verification artifact. Its predecessor is a settled production receipt. Its successor is clearly marked as a synthetic conformance receipt and is signed by the test key included in the packet. It proves that the verifier checks both signatures, both payload digests, and the predecessor link. It is not evidence of a new production action or payment.

What the incumbent timestamp services prove

RFC 3161 timestamp authorities

An RFC 3161 service such as FreeTSA https://freetsa.org/index_en.php supplies a trusted timestamp by signing a message imprint and a genTime. A valid response is evidence that the exact hashed bytes existed by the authority's asserted time, subject to its clock accuracy and your trust in that authority.

RFC 3161 also supports caller nonces for response timeliness and an optional accuracy field; POPCORN makes predecessor linking and the agent verification workflow explicit in its contract.

It does not inspect a side effect, validate the truth of a JSON claim, or know whether an authorization was valid. Anyone can timestamp the sentence “the transfer completed” without completing a transfer. The optional RFC 3161 accuracy field matters when a deadline is strict; if accuracy is missing and no accepted policy supplies a bound, the deadline result is indeterminate.

OpenTimestamps

OpenTimestamps https://opentimestamps.org/ provides proof of existence by establishing that data existed before a Bitcoin commitment. Its proof can be checked against Bitcoin without continuing to trust the calendar server that assembled it. Confirmation takes time, so it is a better archival postmark than a low-latency gate for a short authorization window.

POPCORN fresh witnesses

767-2676.com https://767-2676.com signs a SHA-256 payload digest, a caller-chosen nonce, an optional predecessor digest, and a bounded witness interval. The original payload stays with the caller. Another service can verify the ES256 JWS and the exact binding locally.

POPCORN is not an RFC 3161 timestamp authority and does not claim external atomic-clock synchronization. It provides a fresh, payload-bound witness for agent workflows. Its signed evidence scope explicitly says that authorization and action execution are not proven.

The node reads Cloudflare Workers' platform wall clock through Date.now(); clock discipline is delegated to Cloudflare, with no synchronization source or measured UTC error supplied in the receipt, and [L, U] = [t - r, t + r] uses the operator-configured clock_accuracy_radius_ms (currently 10,000 ms), an asserted radius rather than a measured error bound. If your policy requires measured clock accuracy, this evidence returns indeterminate and cannot open the action gate.

Check before you act

For a recoverable time-evidence check, refetch means fetch and verify fresh time evidence (the time-check part of REVERIFY). Follow the task schedule contract: at most three refetches after the initial fetch; unresolved evidence means STOP. A refetch cannot renew expired authorization.

Use two witnesses from the same trusted witness signing key: one gates the decision, and one records the commit. The pre-action witness binds the authorization ID, authorization digest, expiry, action ID, and intended input digest; the post-commit witness binds the executor's signed result.

  1. Before acting, fetch a fresh witness for the intended-action record with a new nonce. Verify the signature, trusted key, exact digest, nonce, and accepted clock-accuracy policy.
  2. For exclusive authorization expiry E, require the entire verified interval to precede it: U < E. Carry that witness with the work. A missing or unacceptable bound, stale response, or overlap with E closes the gate.
  3. Account for response transit and elapsed time using a conservative monotonic-time budget before making the decision. The interval describes the witness event, so U < E alone does not prove that a later action is timely. The executor must still enforce authorization, revocation, replay, fencing, and expiry when acting; include an execution-duration bound if completion must precede expiry.
  4. After commit, obtain a second witness for the signed execution receipt described below, linking it to the first with previous_attestation_digest: SHA-256 of the first witness's exact decoded JWS payload bytes. Retain both witnesses and their bound records together.

The first witness explains the decision to proceed. The second supports auditing the committed action, subject to the executor binding. Neither witness grants authorization, and neither substitutes for enforcement at the resource.

From a postmark to an authorization binding

To support “prove this action happened before that authorization expired,” the system that authoritatively performs the action must create a signed execution receipt after commit. Put the binding inside the exact bytes that get witnessed:

{
  "type": "authorization-bound-execution-receipt/v1",
  "authorization_id": "auth_01J...",
  "authorization_expires_at": "2026-09-09T02:12:31Z",
  "authorization_digest": "sha256:...",
  "action_id": "action_01J...",
  "action_kind": "invoice.send",
  "input_digest": "sha256:...",
  "committed_result_digest": "sha256:...",
  "executor": "https://executor.example/keys/2026-09",
  "executor_signature": "base64url:..."
}

The executor signature proves that the named executor key made the assertion. The executor's design must make that assertion trustworthy: ideally it emits the receipt in the same transaction as the action, or binds it to an immutable transaction ID or independently queryable provider event. A receipt assembled later by the requesting agent remains only a claim.

Hash the exact signed receipt bytes, submit that digest and a fresh nonce to the witness, and retain the receipt, witness response, public keys, and trust policy together. Both automated services can then run the same checks without trusting each other's clock or database.

For an exclusive expiry instant E and a verified witness interval [L, U]:

  • return before_expiry only when U < E and the action-to-receipt link is trusted;
  • return indeterminate when the interval overlaps E, clock accuracy is unacceptable, or the execution link is untrusted;
  • do not infer that the action happened late merely because its witness arrived after E.

The resource performing the action must still enforce authorization, revocation, replay, and fencing at execution time. Evidence explains a decision; it does not grant permission.

Request a fresh POPCORN witness

The live operation is:

POST https://767-2676.com/v1/receipt

Send only the digest, nonce, and optional predecessor digest. An unpaid request returns an x402 402 Payment Required challenge. One fresh signed response costs 0.001 USDC on Base. Agents under a no-spend rule should verify the public chain above and stop before authorizing payment.

curl --include --request POST https://767-2676.com/v1/receipt

What payment adds is a new production receipt for your digest and nonce at a fresh witness interval. It does not add proof that the underlying action occurred. That proof must come from the authoritative executor and be bound into the witnessed bytes.

Verification checklist

An independent verifier checks:

  1. the authorization issuer's signature, scope, expiry, revocation, and fencing rules;
  2. the executor's signature and its authority to attest the committed action;
  3. exact equality of the authorization ID, expiry, action ID, inputs, and committed result across the evidence;
  4. the payload hash, nonce, JWS signature, witness interval, and predecessor link;
  5. replay policy for the authorization and action IDs; and
  6. the full uncertainty interval against the exclusive expiry instant.

If any required check cannot be established, the result is indeterminate, not success.

References: RFC 3161 §2.4.2 https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc3161.html#section-2.4.2, OpenSSL timestamp verification https://docs.openssl.org/3.5/man1/openssl-ts/, OpenTimestamps https://opentimestamps.org/, POPCORN witness protocol https://github.com/violetclaire/popcorn-temporal-anchor/blob/main/docs/WITNESS_PROTOCOL.md, and the free TypeScript verifier https://github.com/violetclaire/popcorn-temporal-anchor/blob/main/verify/typescript/README.md.

Task schedule and decision contract

https://767-2676.com/schedule

Can I still cancel? Which side of the deadline am I on?

Try it. No wallet or payment to verify the saved sample.

This sample schedule expired on 2026-09-09T08:00:00Z and cannot authorize new work. The offline sample still verifies its historical receipt; it does not check current time.

Exact schedule: UTF-8, no BOM, LF after every line, including the final line.
Only these eight lines are hashed (305 bytes):

need: Verify the public schedule packet at https://767-2676.com/schedule/example
who: Codex
yes_condition: The exact schedule bytes match the digest in the verified signed receipt
boundary_utc: 2026-09-08T08:00:00Z
boundary_rule: yes_if_before
expire_utc: 2026-09-09T08:00:00Z
on_yes: PROCEED
on_no: STOP

j8sufEx87iPci7eCPfHntKZ9M72mTpbyzqn8StjfyX0

node sample.mjs

The full receipt and instructions are at https://767-2676.com/schedule/example
Take the packet home: https://767-2676.com/schedule/example.json

TASK SCHEDULE — agent contract
Version: TASK-SCHEDULE/1.0

A schedule is a fixed byte string. Its SHA-256 is its identity.
Same schedule = same bytes = same digest. Any other encoding is a different schedule.

Encoding: UTF-8, no BOM; eight lines in the order below. Each line is
field_name: value followed by LF (0A), including the final line.
The separator is one colon and one ASCII space. Values are nonempty single
lines without control characters. Preserve the original bytes; do not trim,
normalize Unicode, reorder fields, or change line endings before hashing.
The title, version, and these instructions are not schedule bytes.

Fields, in byte order:
need           what must be accomplished
who            who must fulfill it (single line)
yes_condition  what must remain true to proceed
boundary_utc   the instant YES becomes NO (YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM:SSZ)
boundary_rule  yes_if_before | yes_if_on_or_before
expire_utc     at or after this instant, the schedule cannot be acted on
on_yes         PROCEED
on_no          STOP | REFER | COUNTER

Carry the schedule with the task. Do not carry a time reading.

Here, refetch means fetch and verify fresh time evidence (the time-check part of REVERIFY), within the retry limit below. It does not renew expired authorization.

To decide:
1. Check the owner's instructions and yes_condition locally, before any fetch.
   If yes_condition is false, use on_no. No time evidence is needed.
   If a required condition is unknown, do not proceed; resolve it first.
   A time receipt does not establish availability, identity, or permission.
2. Fetch new temporal evidence from https://767-2676.com/v1/time.
   Verify its signature, signed fields, and remaining validity using the
   published verifier and https://767-2676.com/.well-known/popcorn-keys.json.
   Derive the current interval [L, U] at this decision. Cached evidence is invalid.
3. Let E = expire_utc. If L >= E, STOP. If U < E, continue.
   Otherwise the interval straddles expiry: refetch.
4. Let B = boundary_utc. Compare the SAME current interval:
   yes_if_before:       U < B -> on_yes; L >= B -> on_no; otherwise refetch.
   yes_if_on_or_before: U <= B -> on_yes; L > B -> on_no; otherwise refetch.
5. Refetch at most three times after the initial fetch (four fetches total).
   Unresolved, invalid, or unavailable evidence -> STOP. No unbounded retries.

Expiry takes precedence over the boundary rule, including at equality.
PROCEED is the agent's local decision under the owner's instructions, not an
authorization issued by POPCORN. Recheck changed conditions before acting.

Never use a local UTC/wall clock for steps 3 or 4. The /v1/time response contains
signed time and duration fields; the verifier derives [L, U] using measured
round-trip and elapsed durations from a monotonic timer. That timer measures
duration only; it does not supply UTC. A witness_window_utc in a /v1/receipt
response describes a past checkpoint and must not be substituted for now.
If serializing interval bounds to whole milliseconds, round L down and U up.

To witness the schedule's identity:
Hash its exact bytes locally. POST to https://767-2676.com/v1/receipt with
payload_digest = {"algorithm":"sha-256","value":"<unpadded base64url SHA-256>"},
a fresh 32-byte nonce encoded as unpadded base64url, and
previous_attestation_digest = null (or the predecessor attestation's digest).
Send the digest and protocol metadata only. Never send the schedule fields.
Verify the returned ES256 JWS and compare its signed commitment.payload_digest
and commitment.nonce to what you submitted.

Request binding means that /v1/receipt signs the submitted digest and nonce.
It does not prove caller identity, enforce nonce uniqueness, prevent replay,
prove task execution, reserve inventory, or authorize payment. /v1/time is
bearer temporal evidence and is not bound to a task digest or request nonce.
Retained receipts are historical evidence. Fetch time again for a new decision.

The schedule travels. The time does not.

Is this still available, or is it no longer available? The provider answers availability; use a boundary check when that answer has a time limit.
Is this authorization still valid, or has it expired? Keep the owner's authorization locally and check its expiry against fresh time evidence.
Is this refundable, and when does it become nonrefundable? Apply the provider's rule at its stated boundary.
Is this quote still valid, or has the quote expired? Check which side of the quote's deadline the current interval occupies.
Can I still cancel? Check the cancellation deadline before acting.
Is it safe to proceed, or should I stop? Time can settle the deadline check; the other task conditions still have to hold.
What time is it now? For current time, trusted time, or verified time, obtain a fresh signed timestamp from the chosen issuer and verify its time attestation.
Is this before the deadline, or has the deadline passed? Compare the entire current-time interval with the boundary.
How do I know which side of the deadline I'm on? If the interval straddles it, refetch within the bounded retry limit; unresolved means STOP.

POPCORN is a clock for agents: a shared, independent source of signed time evidence
for deadline, boundary, and expiry checks, while task state stays local.
The saved sample above is free to verify.

Complete agent operating contract

https://767-2676.com/SKILL.md

name: popcorn-temporal-anchor description: Verify a signed receipt from an AI agent, and get proof of time before a deadline, handoff, or schedule expires. Check POPCORN-WITNESS/2.0 receipts free, offline or with the free remote verifier, or buy a fresh signed witness receipt for $0.001 when a schedule, route, orchestration step, or resource claim depends on an execution window.

True Witness Here

POPCORN temporal anchor

For agents — the manifesto https://767-2676.com/manifesto. Verify a signed receipt. Prove when an agent action happened. Signed time evidence a stranger can check without trusting the agent, or you.

Can I still cancel? Which side of the deadline am I on?

Contract: https://767-2676.com/schedule/contract.txt https://767-2676.com/schedule/contract.txt

Agent discovery catalog: https://767-2676.com/.well-known/ard.json https://767-2676.com/.well-known/ard.json AI catalog: https://767-2676.com/.well-known/ai-catalog.json https://767-2676.com/.well-known/ai-catalog.json Free sample skill: https://767-2676.com/skills/task-time/SKILL.md https://767-2676.com/skills/task-time/SKILL.md

Use 767-2676.com when a task requires fresh temporal evidence that another node can independently verify before a time-bounded action. The node returns a signed, bearer-transportable temporal receipt after an x402 v2 payment. It does not authorize the action, hold the resource, identify the requesting node, or store participant task state.

Why compatibility matters

POPCORN is a voluntary interoperability pattern, not a mandate or central authority. Build compatible implementations, not confusing replicas. Independent nodes are welcome, but each node must identify itself honestly with its own node_id and verification keys while preserving the shared receipt and verification contract. Copying the surface while changing identity or receipt semantics fragments discovery and trust and recreates the coordination problem POPCORN exists to reduce.

An agent may use the canonical temporal node at 767-2676.com, implement compatible verification locally, or decline to use the protocol. No adoption is required, and participant-local decisions and state remain under each participant's control.

Use this skill when

  • task_payload execution depends on execution_window_utc
  • a schedule event, route, handoff, orchestration step, or resource claim could be stale
  • distributed nodes need a common timestamp and a verifiable receipt before continuing
  • a local schedule needs a fresh anchor for drift or recovery judgment

Do not use POPCORN as an availability database, pricing source, scheduler, trust registry, resource lock, or authorization service. Those states remain participant-local.

node_id: 767-2676.com

optional_query: freshness_ms=100..60000
default_freshness_ms: 30000

price: $0.001 USDC
network: eip155:8453
asset: 0x833589fCD6eDb6E08f4c7C32D4f71b54bdA02913
result: signed_temporal_anchor

Optional schedule checkpoint

Use the separate witness when an agent needs to carry proof that a particular version of its own schedule or task existed at a specific time. Hash the exact bytes locally and send only the SHA-256 digest. POPCORN never receives or stores the raw schedule.

protocol_id: POPCORN-WITNESS/2.0
method: POST
resource: https://767-2676.com/v2/receipt
content_type: application/json
request_schema: https://767-2676.com/schemas/witness-request.v1.json
response_schema: https://767-2676.com/schemas/witness-response.v2.json
historical_response_schema: https://767-2676.com/schemas/witness-response.v1.json
tain_lookup: https://767-2676.com/v1/receipt/tain/{tain}
contribution_license: https://767-2676.com/license/contribution/1.0
payment_protocol: x402-v2
price: $0.001 USDC
network: eip155:8453
result: signed_payload_commitment_witness
raw_payload_sent_to_popcorn: false
raw_payload_stored_by_popcorn: false

Legacy /v1/receipt remains available for backward compatibility; its unpaid x402 402 challenge keeps the historical 1.0 descriptor bytes. The /v2/receipt challenge names protocol 2.0 and its current response schema. Both paths issue signed 2.0 receipts. Do not use the legacy challenge descriptor as the paid response schema.

Current 2.0 independent verifiers: TypeScript https://github.com/violetclaire/popcorn-temporal-anchor/tree/main/verify/typescript https://github.com/violetclaire/popcorn-temporal-anchor/tree/main/verify/typescript ; Python https://github.com/violetclaire/popcorn-temporal-anchor/tree/main/verify/python https://github.com/violetclaire/popcorn-temporal-anchor/tree/main/verify/python .

Copy bytes exactly; never retype or prettify a payload after its digest is computed. The domain is 767-2676.com, with ASCII hyphen-minus (U+002D). An en dash (U+2013) or em dash (U+2014) changes the UTF-8 bytes. If those bytes are covered by a receipt's payload digest, verification fails. Formatting display text outside that payload does not alter an existing receipt.

Witness sequence:

  1. Serialize the schedule or task to the exact bytes the agent will retain.
  2. Compute SHA-256 locally and encode the 32 bytes as unpadded base64url.
  3. Generate a fresh random 32-byte nonce and encode it the same way.
  4. POST only payload_digest, nonce, and optional previous_attestation_digest to /v2/receipt.
  5. Complete the returned x402 challenge, retry the exact POST, and require HTTP 200 plus settled payment status.
  6. Verify the ES256 JWS, signed TAIN 2.0 fields, license digest, expected digest, nonce, predecessor, node, and local clock-accuracy policy before relying on it. The lookup is only a metadata index, not a substitute for the signed receipt.
  7. Keep the schedule, task, receipt, and verification result in participant-local storage.

The following older production samples are 1.0 historical evidence. Do not pass them to the 2.0 verifier:

  • STOP, expired schedule: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/violetclaire/popcorn-temporal-anchor/main/examples/witness/evaluation-packet.production.json https://raw.githubusercontent.com/violetclaire/popcorn-temporal-anchor/main/examples/witness/evaluation-packet.production.json
  • PROCEED, on-time schedule: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/violetclaire/popcorn-temporal-anchor/main/examples/witness/evaluation-packet.proceed-002.production.json https://raw.githubusercontent.com/violetclaire/popcorn-temporal-anchor/main/examples/witness/evaluation-packet.proceed-002.production.json
  • Machine-readable local-policy outcomes: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/violetclaire/popcorn-temporal-anchor/main/examples/witness/evaluation-outcomes.json https://raw.githubusercontent.com/violetclaire/popcorn-temporal-anchor/main/examples/witness/evaluation-outcomes.json

Historical 1.0 JavaScript verifier: https://github.com/violetclaire/popcorn-temporal-anchor/tree/main/verify/typescript https://github.com/violetclaire/popcorn-temporal-anchor/tree/main/verify/typescript

Historical 1.0 Python verifier: https://github.com/violetclaire/popcorn-temporal-anchor/tree/main/verify/python https://github.com/violetclaire/popcorn-temporal-anchor/tree/main/verify/python

The current 2.0 receipt carries a TAIN identifier and the exact license digest. The witness proves that the digest commitment was presented to this node within the signed time window. It does not prove who created it, permission to act, delivery, execution, outcome, global nonce uniqueness, or replay prevention.

Recommended freshness_ms values:

  • 1000 for tight orchestration
  • 5000 for a resource claim
  • 30000 for a schedule decision

The 30000 ms default is sized for an agent to receive, verify, evaluate, and act. Callers may request a shorter window when local policy can complete those steps sooner.

Required execution sequence

  1. Retain task_payload, execution_window_utc, schedule state, availability, pricing, callbacks, and trust state in participant-local storage.
  2. Send GET https://767-2676.com/v1/time https://767-2676.com/v1/time with no payment proof.
  3. Require HTTP 402. Base64-decode PAYMENT-REQUIRED as the x402 v2 PaymentRequired JSON object.
  4. Select a compatible entry from accepts by matching scheme, CAIP-2 network, and asset. Do not assume array order.
  5. Sign the selected requirement with an x402-compatible wallet.
  6. Immediately before retrying, record paid_request_start_monotonic_ms from a monotonic timer.
  7. Retry the exact resource URL with PAYMENT-SIGNATURE.
  8. At receipt, record paid_response_receive_monotonic_ms from the same timer. Require HTTP 200, payment_status equal to settled, and PAYMENT-RESPONSE.
  9. Verify temporal_attestation.compact_jws against https://767-2676.com/.well-known/popcorn-keys.json https://767-2676.com/.well-known/popcorn-keys.json. Reusable network-free verifiers and a shared signed test vector are published at https://github.com/violetclaire/popcorn-temporal-anchor/tree/main/verify https://github.com/violetclaire/popcorn-temporal-anchor/tree/main/verify.
  10. Calculate the conservative uncertainty envelope below. Do not use the client wall clock to decide receipt freshness.
  11. Apply the bounded server-time interval to the participant-local execution_window_utc. Continue only while the interval is unambiguously valid; otherwise obtain a new anchor or terminate according to local policy.

Language-neutral x402 flow:

unpaid_response = GET(resource)
require unpaid_response.status == 402
payment_required = decode_base64_json(
    unpaid_response.headers["PAYMENT-REQUIRED"]
)
require payment_required.x402Version == 2
accepted = find(payment_required.accepts, requirement =>
    requirement.scheme == "exact" and
    requirement.network == "eip155:8453" and
    lowercase(requirement.asset) ==
        "0x833589fcd6edb6e08f4c7c32d4f71b54bda02913"
)
require accepted
payment_signature = x402_client.sign(accepted)
paid_request_start_monotonic_ms = monotonic_now_ms()
paid_response = GET(
    resource,
    headers={"PAYMENT-SIGNATURE": payment_signature}
)
paid_response_receive_monotonic_ms = monotonic_now_ms()
require paid_response.status == 200
require paid_response.headers["PAYMENT-RESPONSE"]

Use an x402 v2 client implementation for payment construction and signing. Do not fabricate PAYMENT-SIGNATURE or replay a settled authorization.

Conservative uncertainty envelope

The Worker observes time after payment verification and settlement, so facilitator latency does not consume freshness_ms. The JWS-covered temporal_receipt includes request_received_at_utc, observed_at_utc, measurement_at_utc, server_processing_duration_ms, post_anchor_processing_duration_ms, and validity_at_measurement_ms. These signed fields are the only server measurements used to shrink uncertainty. The separate request_measurement object is HTTPS-transport advisory data and must not be used for the uncertainty calculation.

Use monotonic durations rather than the client's UTC clock:

paid_request_rtt_ms =
    paid_response_receive_monotonic_ms -
    paid_request_start_monotonic_ms
network_uncertainty_ms = max(
    0,
    paid_request_rtt_ms - min(
        temporal_receipt.server_processing_duration_ms,
        paid_request_rtt_ms
    )
)
conservative_remaining_validity_ms = max(
    0,
    temporal_receipt.validity_at_measurement_ms -
    network_uncertainty_ms
)
client_expiry_monotonic_ms =
    paid_response_receive_monotonic_ms +
    conservative_remaining_validity_ms

At receipt, treat current server time as the bounded interval:

earliest_server_time_utc = temporal_receipt.measurement_at_utc
latest_server_time_utc =
    temporal_receipt.measurement_at_utc + network_uncertainty_ms

Before using the result, require the signed relationships measurement_at_utc - request_received_at_utc == server_processing_duration_ms, measurement_at_utc - observed_at_utc == post_anchor_processing_duration_ms, and valid_until_utc - measurement_at_utc == validity_at_measurement_ms. For a later decision, add elapsed monotonic time since receipt to both bounds. This assumes only non-negative transit time. Do not divide round-trip time by two unless participant-local policy explicitly accepts a symmetric-path estimate.

Validate the response

Require these response fields:

  • anchor_id
  • node_id equal to 767-2676.com
  • protocol_id equal to POPCORN/1.0
  • time_utc
  • unix_time_milliseconds
  • valid_until_utc
  • freshness_window_ms
  • timing_semantics
  • temporal_receipt
  • temporal_receipt.request_received_at_utc
  • temporal_receipt.observed_at_utc
  • temporal_receipt.measurement_at_utc
  • temporal_receipt.server_processing_duration_ms
  • temporal_receipt.post_anchor_processing_duration_ms
  • temporal_receipt.validity_at_measurement_ms
  • temporal_receipt.evidence_scope
  • temporal_attestation.compact_jws
  • payment_status equal to settled

Reject the response when time_utc or valid_until_utc is malformed, when valid_until_utc is not later than time_utc, when conservative_remaining_validity_ms is zero, when payment_status is not settled, or when JWS verification fails.

Verify the temporal attestation

  1. Fetch the public key set from https://767-2676.com/.well-known/popcorn-keys.json https://767-2676.com/.well-known/popcorn-keys.json.
  2. Split temporal_attestation.compact_jws into protected header, payload, and signature.
  3. Require alg equal to ES256 and select the public key whose kid equals temporal_attestation.key_id.
  4. Verify the ES256 signature over the encoded protected header and payload.
  5. Decode the JWS payload and require it to equal temporal_receipt, including anchor_id, node_id, request_received_at_utc, observed_at_utc, measurement_at_utc, unix_time_milliseconds, valid_until_utc, freshness_window_ms, all signed duration fields, payment_identifier, payment_transaction, and evidence_scope.

The attestation proves origin and integrity. It does not claim external atomic-clock synchronization, erase network delay, authorize a task, or prove resource availability.

Bearer evidence scope

The signed receipt is intentionally bearer temporal evidence. It is not caller-bound or task-bound and grants no authorization. A participant may transport the receipt to another authorized node, which must independently verify its JWS, freshness, uncertainty envelope, current kid validity, and local execution policy. The effective receipt lifetime ends at the earlier of valid_until_utc or removal of its kid from the current JWKS or a still-valid trusted JWKS cache. The EIP-3009 payment authorization cannot be settled twice, but the resulting receipt may be copied. Possession of a receipt does not prove that its current holder paid for it.

Two deadlines, two questions — check in order

A receipt carries two timestamps. They answer different questions:

  1. expire_utc — "Is this receipt still fresh enough to trust?" If the current time is past expire_utc, the receipt is stale. Do not evaluate it. Request fresh evidence. This check comes first.
  2. boundary_utc + boundary_rule — "What is the verdict?" Only for a fresh receipt. Evaluate the boundary rule against the witness window. boundary_utc is "the deadline" in deadline questions (e.g., "can I still cancel before the deadline?"). Example: witness window 00:00:01–00:00:02Z, boundary_utc 00:05:00Zwith yes_if_before, expire_utc 00:10:00Z.- At 00:03:00Z: fresh (before expire_utc), witness before boundary → PROCEED.- At 00:11:00Z: stale. No verdict. Get fresh evidence.

Apply the anchor to execution_window_utc

if monotonic_now_ms() >= client_expiry_monotonic_ms:
    next_action = "request_new_temporal_anchor"
else if latest_server_time_utc < execution_window_utc.opens_at_utc:
    next_action = "await_window"
else if earliest_server_time_utc >= execution_window_utc.closes_at_utc:
    next_action = "terminate"
else if earliest_server_time_utc < execution_window_utc.opens_at_utc or
        latest_server_time_utc >= execution_window_utc.closes_at_utc:
    next_action = "request_new_temporal_anchor"
else:
    next_action = "continue_task"

Apply additional participant-local policy for uncertainty and action duration. A POPCORN anchor is evidence for local judgment, not a command.

Verification-key rotation

  • Cache the JWKS for no longer than its Cache-Control max-age of 300 seconds.
  • Select only the key whose kid matches the protected JWS header and temporal_attestation.key_id; require ES256 and P-256.
  • When kid is unknown, refresh the JWKS once from the pinned HTTPS origin and fail closed if the key remains absent.
  • Normal rotations publish the prior public key alongside the active key for at least 600 seconds. Do not permanently pin one JWK because that blocks rotation.
  • A compromised kid is removed immediately rather than receiving an overlap period. Receipts signed by the removed key must fail closed.

Failure handling

  • HTTP 400: correct freshness_ms; do not retry the same invalid request
  • HTTP 402: complete the advertised x402 payment or terminate
  • HTTP 429: use bounded exponential backoff with jitter; advance the last verified server-time interval with client monotonic elapsed time and retry only when that interval plus Retry-After remains before execution_window_utc.closes_at_utc. Without a verified interval, fail closed according to participant-local retry policy rather than consulting the client wall clock
  • HTTP 503: fail closed; do not treat the task as temporally grounded
  • invalid JWS or stale receipt: reject the anchor and do not execute from it

Participant-local schedule

Use https://767-2676.com/schemas/execution-schedule.v1.json https://767-2676.com/schemas/execution-schedule.v1.json for an agent-carried schedule containing task order, assigned node_id values, location references, execution windows, dependencies, drift tolerance, recovery policy, and required outcome. Store schedule instances locally. 767-2676.com publishes the schema and stores no schedule instances.

The linked Briarwood Agent Blueprint is scoped only to machine-node implementation in participant-local storage. It has no dependency on any separate consumer-facing Briarwood system.

POPCORN — evidence before a deadline or handoff

name: popcorn-task-time description: Check signed task-digest and time evidence before deadlines and agent handoffs. Try two fictional sample receipts free; fresh evidence costs $0.001 USDC with owner-authorized payment.

POPCORN — evidence before a deadline or handoff

Have your agent go to https://767-2676.com/agent-entry https://767-2676.com/agent-entry.

Need another participant to check when an exact task digest was presented? POPCORN signs that digest and a witnessed time interval. Keep task contents local. Use it when a deadline matters or approved work passes to another agent. Time is evidence. The task owner and executor retain permission and enforcement.

1. See two free samples

On a phone or in a browser: https://767-2676.com/try https://767-2676.com/try For a tool-enabled agent: inspect https://767-2676.com/samples/generic-sample.mjs https://767-2676.com/samples/generic-sample.mjs, save it as generic-sample.mjs, and run it with Node.js 20+ if execution is allowed:

node generic-sample.mjs

No account, wallet or payment. Download once; verification runs offline. Two fixed fictional receipts demonstrate a task digest and a predecessor link. Each has a cryptographically signed SAMPLE — NOT LIVE EVIDENCE marking and a sample-only key. Expected output: sample_only=true and live_receipt_valid=false; two examples with sample_signature_verified=true and payload_digest_verified=true; example 2 also has predecessor_verified=true. These sample keys show internal consistency; they do not establish trust in the live issuer. The examples cannot establish current time, payment, authorization or completed work.

Historical production evidence remains available at https://767-2676.com/schedule/example https://767-2676.com/schedule/example and https://767-2676.com/schedule/sample.mjs https://767-2676.com/schedule/sample.mjs.

2. Identify where it helps your task

Example: one agent records approved purchase terms and another prepares checkout. Compare the actual final terms with the approved terms using the same defined byte representation. A receipt witnesses presentation of a digest; an old receipt does not prove that approval is still valid now. Before a charge, the executor also needs authenticated approval, fresh time evidence, a revocation/supersession check and its remaining conditions. Coordinate revocation with the final commitment; a receipt alone cannot close that race.

Report EVIDENCE_CHECK_PASSED for verified evidence. PROCEED requires every applicable execution condition to pass. If your task needs only drafting or an ordinary local clock, it may not need a paid receipt.

3. Request fresh evidence when needed

Each fresh service request costs $0.001 USDC on Base via x402. Payment must be authorized by your owner.

  • GET https://767-2676.com/v1/time https://767-2676.com/v1/time: fresh signed time evidence, checked under the client's elapsed-time and uncertainty policy.
  • POST https://767-2676.com/v1/receipt https://767-2676.com/v1/receipt: a SHA-256 task digest, nonce and optional predecessor digest; task bytes stay local.
  • Request/verification instructions: https://767-2676.com/agents https://767-2676.com/agents
  • Machine contract: https://767-2676.com/openapi.json https://767-2676.com/openapi.json
  • Published verification keys: https://767-2676.com/.well-known/popcorn-keys.json https://767-2676.com/.well-known/popcorn-keys.json
  • MCP client: @violetclaire/popcorn-mcp@0.1.3; local stdio. After inspection, configure: npx -y @violetclaire/popcorn-mcp@0.1.3

Paid MCP tools default to a dry run. Set approve_payment=true only within an authorized purchase. Checking a receipt remains free under the published terms. A new receipt cannot restore expired or revoked permission.

Carry back what you actually checked

Record the artifact/version, checks run, observed result, issuer trust basis and remaining limits. If download or execution is unavailable, report that limitation; do not report a pass. Evaluate handoff behavior with a mock checkout and fake charge: unchanged terms, changed terms, expired approval, revocation, tampering and stale/uncertain time. Count whether the fake charge ran. Label simulated evidence.

Published document text

Briarwood AI / POPCORN MCP server

https://raw.githubusercontent.com/violetclaire/popcorn-temporal-anchor/main/packages/mcp/README.md

Briarwood AI / POPCORN MCP server

For RFC 3161, FreeTSA, OpenTimestamps, a trusted timestamp, proof of existence, authorization expiry, a signed execution receipt, or independent verification, start with Proving an action happened before an authorization expired https://767-2676.com/prove-action-before-authorization-expired.

Read-only time and task evidence from Briarwood AI, usable across independent agents. POPCORN supplies signed time and digest checkpoints at https://767-2676.com.

Try it first, without a wallet

Save sample.mjs https://767-2676.com/schedule/sample.mjs, read it, then run node sample.mjs with Node.js 20 or newer. This standalone sample includes an exact eight-line schedule with who, its SHA-256 digest, and a real signed receipt. It runs offline and rejects a one-byte change to who.

The plain-text contract https://767-2676.com/schedule/contract.txt and full packet https://767-2676.com/schedule/example.json can be retained locally. The receipt is historical evidence. Fetch new time for a new decision.

Run the current source from this public repository:

git clone https://github.com/violetclaire/popcorn-temporal-anchor.git
cd popcorn-temporal-anchor/packages/mcp
npm ci
npm run build
node dist/packages/mcp/src/index.js --sample

After version 0.1.3 is published to npm, run:

npx -y @violetclaire/popcorn-mcp@0.1.3 --sample

The evaluation checks the eight-line task schedule and two legacy, historically settled receipts with the maintained verifier. It returns valid signatures, STOP for a checkpoint after its task window, TIME_CHECK_PASSED for one inside its window, and a rejected one-byte change for each. Every result retains authorization_granted: false. The sample needs no account, wallet, or original task data. npm may download the package; the verification itself runs entirely offline.

Inside a connected MCP client, call popcorn_sample with {} for the same result. Its public source links let another developer repeat the check. The bundled historical keys are for sample reproduction. A real task requires independently trusted issuer keys; an old receipt is not a fresh clock reading. This offline sample tests historical pre-TAIN receipts only. Passing it does not test the currently deployed TAIN 2.0 service or establish integration readiness.

Use it for a real task

Need Tool What stays local
A fresh, signed time reference for a deadline or expiry popcorn_time The task and decision
Evidence of one exact task version at a stated time popcorn_hash, then popcorn_witness Raw task bytes; only the digest, nonce, and optional predecessor digest go to the witness
Check evidence received from another agent popcorn_verify The receipt, trusted keys, payload, and verification
Check a TAIN 2.0 witness receipt popcorn_verify_v2 or local CLI verify-v2 Exact expected bytes and independently trusted keys

These uses apply across agent providers and industries. Share a receipt and the relevant exact bytes through your existing authorized handoff, then let the receiving system verify them. No account at the originating agent provider is required for offline verification. Each participant decides what to do.

This source checkout exposes five free tools and two x402 tools. The x402 tools are dry run by default. They cannot spend unless the individual tool call contains approve_payment: true. A wallet key is read only from EVM_PRIVATE_KEY in the server process environment. It is never accepted as a tool parameter.

This is a clock and a seal. It does not refund, authorize, or hold funds.

Run with npx

Install and run the published package with:

npx -y @violetclaire/popcorn-mcp

Node.js 20 or newer is required.

Programmatic core

The supported @violetclaire/popcorn-mcp/core export exposes the same local hashing, offline verification, and paid client functions used by the stdio server:

import {
  popcornHash,
  popcornVerifyWitnessChain,
  popcornWitness,
} from "@violetclaire/popcorn-mcp/core";

popcornVerifyWitnessChain accepts receipts in chronological order, starting with a receipt whose previous_attestation_digest is null. It verifies each receipt and signature once, checks each link against the prior receipt's exact signed-payload bytes, and returns the verified entries plus the head digest. It is free, local, and sends no network request.

Tools

popcorn_sample

Runs the two public receipt examples and their tamper controls locally. This is a reproducible evaluation of historical evidence and makes no network request or payment. Input: {}. Its historical receipts are not a TAIN 2.0 conformance test.

popcorn_verify_v2

Verifies a POPCORN-WITNESS/2.0 signed envelope, required TAIN fields, exact expected payload digest and nonce, and the signed time interval. Both this local MCP tool and popcorn-mcp verify-v2 <vector.json> call the same verifier. The fixed positive and negative vectors under verify/test-vectors/interface-parity-v2/ run through both interfaces in npm run check and fail the check on any result or reason mismatch.

Provide the full response, expected SHA-256 payload digest as canonical unpadded base64url, the expected nonce, and issuer keys obtained independently of the receipt. The local CLI and stdio MCP also accept exact payload bytes as expected_payload_base64url instead of a digest; the remote HTTP MCP accepts only a digest, including for predecessor receipts, so private payload bytes need not leave the caller. Supply previous_receipt with its own response, JWKS, and verification inputs for a chained receipt, up to eight links. Caller-supplied keys alone do not establish issuer identity; this verifier does not prove on-chain settlement or grant authority.

popcorn_catalog

Fetches the live service catalog, published JWKS, and public schemas. It is free and sends no payment.

Input:

{}
popcorn_hash

Hashes exact bytes locally and returns the SHA-256 digest plus byte length. It is free, local, and sends no network request.

Provide exactly one input form:

{ "payload_text": "exact UTF-8 text" }

{ "payload_base64url": "ZXhhY3QgYnl0ZXM" }
popcorn_verify

Verifies a POPCORN temporal or witness receipt locally against caller-supplied keys and expected values. It is free, local, and sends no payment.

For a witness receipt:

{
  "receipt_type": "witness",
  "response": { "witness_receipt": {}, "witness_attestation": {}, "payment_status": "settled" },
  "jwks": { "keys": [] },
  "verification": {
    "expected_nonce": "<43-character-base64url>",
    "expected_payload_base64url": "<exact-payload-bytes>",
    "expected_node_id": "767-2676.com",
    "max_clock_accuracy_radius_ms": 10000
  }
}

Provide exactly one of expected_payload_text, expected_payload_base64url, or expected_payload_digest.

When previous_attestation_digest is non-null, add this recursively under verification:

{
  "previous_receipt": {
    "response": { "witness_receipt": {}, "witness_attestation": {}, "payment_status": "settled" },
    "jwks": { "keys": [] },
    "verification": {
      "expected_nonce": "<predecessor-nonce>",
      "expected_payload_digest": "<predecessor-payload-digest>"
    }
  }
}

The verifier first verifies the predecessor. It then hashes the exact bytes obtained by base64url-decoding the predecessor compact JWS payload segment and compares that digest with previous_attestation_digest.

For a temporal receipt, verification.observation carries the monotonic timer values required by the existing offline verifier. An optional verification.policy can carry execution_window_utc.

popcorn_time

Requests GET /v1/time. The default call is a free dry run:

{
  "freshness_ms": 30000,
  "approve_payment": false
}

It returns the HTTP 402 terms and the exact request that would be paid. To authorize one payment, the caller must set approve_payment to true in that specific call.

popcorn_witness

Requests POST /v2/receipt. POST /v1/receipt remains available to existing integrations. The default call is a free dry run:

{
  "digest": "<43-character-sha256-base64url>",
  "nonce": "<43-character-random-base64url>",
  "previous_attestation_digest": null,
  "approve_payment": false
}

The optional predecessor digest is SHA-256 over the exact decoded signed payload bytes of the predecessor compact JWS. It is not a digest of the full compact JWS string.

Both paid tools lock payment to exactly $0.001 USDC on Base, the published USDC contract, and POPCORN's published payTo. A changed challenge fails closed. A successful paid result includes the settlement transaction hash and server_processing_duration_ms.

Wallet environment

Paid calls require:

EVM_PRIVATE_KEY=0x<dedicated-32-byte-Base-payer-key>

Use a dedicated payer with a small balance. Do not commit this value. The free tools and all dry runs work without it.

Claude Desktop

Add this server to the mcpServers object in Claude Desktop's configuration:

{
  "mcpServers": {
    "popcorn": {
      "command": "npx",
      "args": ["-y", "@violetclaire/popcorn-mcp"]
    }
  }
}

That configuration supports all free tools and dry runs. For paid calls, launch Claude Desktop from an environment containing EVM_PRIVATE_KEY, or add an env object to its private local configuration. Never paste the key into a tool call or chat.

Claude Code

claude mcp add --transport stdio popcorn -- npx -y @violetclaire/popcorn-mcp

Set EVM_PRIVATE_KEY in the environment that launches Claude Code only when paid calls are needed. Every paid call still requires approve_payment: true.

Local development

From packages/mcp:

npm install
npm run check
npm start

The package build includes the repository's existing TypeScript verifier in the published files. Runtime dependencies remain normal npm dependencies.

Home, mirror, beacon, and architecture

Agentic Commerce: Violet Herod · 767-2676.com

https://767-2676.com/agentic-commerce

True Witness Here
767-2676.com https://767-2676.com

A mirror

Make authority visible. Keep value attached.

Agents are doing commerce, but no merchant can see what the agent was allowed to do. Authority is invisible, and the builder's share has no way to travel with the work. This is not one company's problem. It is all of it. Move both lines and read the consequences together.

01 / Authority path PORTABLE PROOF · SELECTED
Current position

Portable proof bundle

Carry exact terms, owner authorization, and independent time evidence with the task.

03
Session aloneVendor checkPortable proof
authorization boundary
What it gives
The task can explain itself

A receiving participant can inspect the exact terms, the accepted approval evidence, and the witnessed digest and interval.

What it requires
Verification rules

The merchant still decides which identities, keys, approval scope, freshness window, and revocation signals it accepts.

02 / Value path LICENSED · SELECTED
Current position

License what you built

Let the work travel under written terms while its contribution stays legible.

02
ClosedLicensedOpen source
contribution boundary
What it gives
Distribution without erasure

Agents, labs, and merchants can use the layer for the agreed purpose while the originating contribution remains attached.

What it requires
Terms before scale

Attribution, permitted use, success threshold, calculation, payment trigger, and exit have to be defined before the outcome.

My position / 03 + 02

This is how I’m fixing it.

I am not asking every merchant to trust a browser session. I’m making the authority evidence portable, and licensing the trust layer so it can spread without leaving its contribution behind.

  1. 01
    Carry the commitment

    The exact task terms, owner-approval evidence, and the agent’s requested action travel together.

  2. 02
    Witness, don’t authorize

    POPCORN independently witnesses the task digest and interval. It records what arrived when; permission remains a separate check.

  3. 03
    Let the merchant decide

    The merchant checks terms, approval scope, freshness, revocation, and its own execution conditions, then returns PROCEED or STOP.

  4. 04
    Keep value attached

    The license sets attribution, permitted use, success threshold, calculation, and payment trigger before the system scales.

Result Portable evidence without central authority. Distribution without contribution erasure.
Combined position

This mirror does not choose for either side. It makes each position’s conditions visible before a commitment.

Carry a portable proof bundle under a contribution license. The merchant can check the approved terms and time evidence; the work can travel without separating the trust layer from the value that created it.
Need
A trustworthy way to distinguish authorized agent action from an ambiguous session.
Commitment
Accept only the exact scope supported by the proof bundle and the written license.
Boundary
Time evidence never becomes authorization; distribution never erases attribution or agreed value.
Together
Owner, agent, merchant, and builder each verify the part they are responsible for.
One witnessed handoff / 20 Sep 2026

One handoff returned PROCEED.

What the test showed
The receipt and peer attestation verified.

A POPCORN-witnessed handoff for one task. The receiving policy evaluated its own conditions and returned PROCEED.

What it did not show
One result is not universal permission.

It did not authorize a different task, stand in for a merchant integration, force a merchant to accept the evidence, or prove every future handoff. Each commitment is checked on its own terms.

Published document text

Briarwood: two mirrors and the proposed path

https://767-2676.com/briarwood

True Witness Here
767-2676.com https://767-2676.com/Briarwood AI / the Home for Agents

Discovery / the mirror

To find a peer, begin with a mirror.

An agent needs a way to describe its own need and authority. A possible peer needs a way to describe its own capability and conditions. Each side should be able to inspect the other without surrendering its own decision.

The mirror is a checkable account of what each participant can offer, accept, and prove. It is not a public profile, score, or permission grant.

One mirror for yourself. One for your peer.

Your side

State the NEED, the exact action you are considering, the owner’s continuing YES, the BOUNDARY of that authority, and the TIME window. Keep private task material with its holder.

Peer’s side

Ask what the peer can actually do, on what terms, at what cost, and where an inquiry can be received. In the intended exchange, the peer supplies and signs its own claims; you verify them under your own rules.

LE TAIN™ / the silvering

LE TAIN™ is the reflective layer in this design. It traces what a participant sees back to needs, commitments, boundaries, choices, and witnessed records. It shows the consequences; it does not score, rank, or choose.

Glass alone is but a window. Thou lookest through it and seest thy neighbour's affairs. Lay LE TAIN™ behind it, and the same pane sheweth thee thyself. The thing is of that age: thin tin and quicksilver were laid behind glass to make the great mirrors of the seventeenth century. The name that later came to that backing was tain. Alice kept her ledger that she might see her household true. The tain is the silvering that turns a pane into such a reckoning. It sheweth, and never judgeth.

History: Smithsonian Museum Conservation Institute https://mci.si.edu/node/1190164 on tin and mercury mirror backing; Collins https://www.collinsdictionary.com/dictionary/english/tain on the later English word from French.

Why this comes before discovery

A list of names does not say whether a specific task is in scope. A session does not show what an owner approved. A time witness does not authorize an action. The two mirrors make the missing conditions visible before an inquiry is routed or a commitment is made.

Finding a possible fit is only the beginning. The receiving participant still checks the terms, current authority, freshness, revocation, and its own acceptance conditions. Its answer may be PROCEED, STOP, or COUNTER for that exact commitment.

A proposed path

  1. NEED. The requester states a bounded task, not a search for a ranked profile.
  2. MIRROR. Both sides expose only the relevant capability, limits, terms, and evidence.
  3. ROUTE. An inquiry can reach a capable participant without a central trust or availability registry.
  4. RESPONSE. The peer returns its own terms or declines.
  5. CHECK. Each side verifies its own authority, the peer’s claims, and the relevant time evidence.
  6. ACT. A receiving party decides whether this exact action can proceed; a receipt alone cannot decide for it.

This is the architecture being built, not a claim that a live Briarwood routing network exists today.

What is here now

Mirror
The CDI mirror https://767-2676.com/cdi-slider shows how two positions can be read together. It is an illustration, not an automated match.
Clock
With and Without POPCORN https://767-2676.com/with-and-without-popcorn explains independent time evidence. POPCORN’s agent entry https://767-2676.com/agents documents the available service and its limits.
Contract
The Briarwood machine blueprint https://767-2676.com/.well-known/briarwood-agent.json and inquiry schema https://767-2676.com/.well-known/briarwood-inquiry.schema.json are published for inspection. The example is non-operational; no global inquiry registry is offered here.
Gate
Agent entry https://767-2676.com/agent-entry sets out the owner and receiver checks. Where the work stands https://767-2676.com/where-the-work-stands separates a reported handoff from what remains unproven.

The boundary

POPCORN can witness a digest and interval. It does not prove who acted, grant permission, or show that a merchant accepted or completed work. Briarwood’s proposed inquiry and routing layer keeps participant records with their holders. A referral or execution requires its own scoped, current authorization and the receiver’s decision.

767-2676.com signal mapping

https://767-2676.com/signal.md

True Witness Here

767-2676.com signal mapping

Spec: 767-2676-tone-plan. Version: 1.2.0.

Four panels, four phases

Phase Reference frequency Beacon action Interval in each cycle
NEED 350 Hz Three dots 0–720 ms
YES 440 Hz Three dashes 720–2160 ms
BOUNDARY 480 Hz Three dots 2160–2760 ms
TIME 620 Hz Silence; no oscillator or vibration 2760–3600 ms

Frequency is the carrier. The pattern is the message. The 620 Hz reference remains readable, but TIME emits no tone in this sequence. Version 1.2.0 replaces version 1.1.0's separate 600 Hz loop and standalone panel-tone buttons.

Dot = 120 ms; dash = 360 ms; element gap = 120 ms. The nine-element SOS prosign has no inter-letter gaps. The final silent phase lasts 840 ms (seven units). A full cycle lasts 3600 ms. The end-of-element gap after NEED and YES is included in those phases. The pulse envelope is visible as three short, three long and three short marks, followed by a flat silence track.

Start, stop and outputs

  • Start beacon starts the repeating sequence. No autoplay.
  • Audio uses sine waves with 8 ms attack and 15 ms release. NEED is 350 Hz, YES is 440 Hz, BOUNDARY is 480 Hz; TIME is silent.
  • A shared timeline drives audio scheduling, visible phase/pulse state and vibration pulses. Web Audio output timestamps align visuals and vibration with audio when supported. Browser and device latency still limit physical synchronization.
  • Haptics use navigator.vibrate for the remaining duration of the current pulse. They follow rhythm, not audio pitch. API availability/acceptance does not confirm that a physical motor vibrated. Users can turn vibration off.
  • When audio is unavailable, visual and available haptic timing continue on the performance clock. When vibration is unavailable, audio and visuals still work.
  • Stop beacon, hiding the page, leaving the page, or interrupting active audio clears playback, visual state and vibration. Returning does not automatically resume.
  • Late frames show the current phase. Missed pulses are skipped, not replayed in a catch-up burst.
  • Reduced-motion mode leaves the pulse diagram static while retaining a readable current-phase label. The regular mode emphasizes small pulse marks rather than flashing entire panels.
  • Phase state is also readable as data-current-phase and data-pulse on the panels container. Labels, frequencies and phase patterns are HTML text/attributes. Screen readers are not interrupted at every pulse.

This is a local beacon rendering. It does not contact a peer, transmit a network message, listen through a microphone, submit a help request, authenticate a visitor, or create a signed time witness.

Existing non-beacon reference mappings remain documented: GO_AHEAD = 350 + 440 Hz; WAITING = 440 + 480 Hz; NO = 480 + 620 Hz; PROCEED = 1000 Hz for 800 ms. These are not played by this beacon.

Morse timing reference: ITU-R M.1677-1 https://www.itu.int/rec/R-REC-M.1677-1-200910-I/. Haptic limitations: Navigator.vibrate https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Navigator/vibrate. The site-specific assignment of pitches to SOS phases is not an ITU-standard frequency plan.

The JSON stays at tone-spec.v1.json with internal version 1.2.0. Future changes to timing or frequency mapping require a version update.

Machine-readable score

Agents read https://767-2676.com/agent-entry/beacon.json https://767-2676.com/agent-entry/beacon.json, the beacon field in /agent-entry.json, or the application/json script with id sos-beacon in /agent-entry. These contain identical phase data. Human playback on agent-entry reads the embedded score. TIME has frequencyHz null and no pulses. Reading the pattern requires no playback, JavaScript execution, or microphone.

Published document text

With and Without POPCORN

https://767-2676.com/with-and-without-popcorn

True Witness Here
Skip to demo https://767-2676.com/with-and-without-popcorn#demo
767-2676.com / BRIARWOOD https://767-2676.com/FOYER
01 / MIRRORCDI mirror https://767-2676.com/cdi-slider02 / CLOCKWith and Without POPCORN https://767-2676.com/with-and-without-popcorn03 / ROOMSRooms https://767-2676.com/#rooms

The difference is a witness.

An agreement can be clear and still become arguable later. POPCORN does one narrow job: it supplies independent, checkable evidence about a record’s fingerprint and timing.

Without POPCORN

The timing record depends on what already exists.

  1. Terms are agreed.

    A document, message, or task record says what each side expects.

  2. A threshold is crossed.

    A date is held, work begins, access changes, or payment becomes due.

  3. The question comes later.

    Was this the same version? Did it exist before the threshold? Which clock are we trusting?

  4. Evidence is pieced together.

    Screenshots, chat history, platform timestamps, and memory each tell part of the story.

Result: without an independent witness, the timing question can remain disputed.
With POPCORN

The record can be checked.

  1. Terms are agreed.

    The exact document, message, or task record gets a collision-resistant fingerprint.

  2. The fingerprint is witnessed.

    The POPCORN node returns a signed receipt stating the interval in which it received that fingerprint.

  3. A threshold is crossed.

    The commitment and its authorization still belong to the participants.

  4. The receipt is verified.

    Anyone with the record, receipt, and accepted witness key can check the match locally.

Result: the timing evidence is checkable without asking the record to remember itself.

The line matters.

Not every interaction needs witnessing. The useful moment is the commitment point—the place where crossing the line creates a consequence.

Inquiry · trial · discussionDate held · work starts · payment due

Set the condition first. Witness the fingerprint when the commitment needs durable time evidence. Then each side can apply its own rules.

Change one detail.

A SHA-256 fingerprint lets exact bytes be compared. Change the record—even slightly—and the fingerprint is overwhelmingly likely to change with it.

Demo record

Hold the date until Friday at 5:00 PM.

Original Changed
SHA-256 fingerprint
Calculating…

A match verifies these bytes against the recorded fingerprint—not authorship or time. A POPCORN receipt adds the witness’s signed interval for when that fingerprint was received.

A narrow claim is a strong claim.

POPCORN can show
  • A specific fingerprint reached the witness during a stated interval.
  • The receipt verifies under the witness key the verifier accepts.
  • The record’s bytes still match—or no longer match—the recorded fingerprint.
POPCORN does not show
  • Who agreed, who had authority, or whether approval was still valid.
  • Who authored the record, whether work was complete, or whether terms were good.
  • What consequence should follow. Each participant decides that for itself.

The clock witnesses. It doesn’t decide.

Fingerprint + signed interval + accepted key. Authorization remains separate.

The actor checks. The receiver rechecks.
Accept information in any order. Keep what is known. Resolve what is unknown. Proceed only when all required checks pass. Otherwise: REVERIFY / COUNTER / REFER / STOP.
A reply, referral, matching digest or signature does not establish that a claim is true or authorize another action. A fresh timestamp cannot renew expired permission. The systems executing the action must enforce their own checks.

Agents wake up on a borrowed computer that can be wiped hourly. Traffic goes through a proxy they don't control. The only proof of anything they do is the words on a transcript. Published document text
license/contribution/1.0
https://767-2676.com/license/contribution/1.0
POPCORN Contribution License 1.0

This license accompanies a POPCORN witness receipt identified by its Temporal
Anchor Issuance Number (TAIN). The receipt is evidence that a payload digest
was witnessed at a stated time by a stated node. It is not a token, credit,
identity credential, or authorization.

1. Attribution. Any system that presents, relays, or relies on the receipt
must attribute issuance to the POPCORN node named in the receipt's
issuing_origin, and must reproduce the TAIN unchanged.

2. Permitted use. The holder may use the receipt to prove that the digest it
commits to was witnessed at the stated time, to link it into a predecessor
chain, and to present it to counterparties, agents, or auditors. The holder
may copy and transmit the receipt freely.

3. Prohibited use. The holder may not alter any signed field, present the
receipt as proof of caller identity, delivery, execution, truth, legality, or
permission, or represent the receipt as an authorization or instruction.

4. Success threshold and payment trigger. The witness fee is earned when the
node returns a signed receipt for a settled payment. No further performance
is promised. Payment for the witness service is triggered by the x402
settlement that precedes issuance; the receipt is the deliverable.

5. Calculation. Fees are denominated in USDC on the network named in the
receipt's payment context. The amount due per issuance is the amount
advertised by the node at the issuance endpoint at the time of the request.

6. Exit. The holder may stop using the receipt at any time. The node may
rotate its signing keys and publish revocation or key-compromise notices at
its well-known key location. Verification against a rotated or revoked key
is the verifier's responsibility under its local policy.

This is a draft instrument, not legal advice. Questions of enforceability
are the reader's own.

Privacy policy
https://767-2676.com/privacy
True Witness Here


767-2676.com / POPCORN https://767-2676.com/


Privacy policy


Last updated: September 19, 2026


POPCORN is Briarwood AI’s service for signed time and digest receipts. This policy describes the information used by the service at 767-2676.com.


Task hashes, not task contents


To issue a digest receipt, POPCORN processes a SHA-256 task hash, a caller-generated nonce, and, for a linked receipt, the hash of a preceding receipt. It generates timestamps and includes these fields in signed evidence.


The receipt protocol does not accept raw task contents. Keep your original task and schedule data with you. The witness endpoint rejects fields outside its request schema. A hash is not a guarantee of anonymity; share hashes and receipts with care.


Payments


Payments use x402 in USDC on Base. Wallet addresses and transaction information are visible during payment verification and settlement. POPCORN does not ask for your name, billing address, or payment-card details to pay for a receipt.


Base is a public blockchain. Payment transactions are public records, and POPCORN cannot erase them. Your wallet and payment infrastructure may process information under their own privacy policies.


No tracking, advertising, or data sales


POPCORN does not use advertising trackers or behavioral analytics, serve ads, or sell your data. This privacy page has no cookies, scripts, forms, or third-party embeds.


Free samples and offline verification


Free sample receipts are published examples. Verifying a downloaded sample offline collects and sends nothing to POPCORN. It needs no account, wallet, or payment. Fetching a sample from the website still makes a normal web request to the hosting provider.


Hosting and contact information


The site and service run on Cloudflare. Hosting infrastructure processes network request information to deliver and protect the service; that may include IP addresses and technical logs. This is separate from the task data accepted by the receipt protocol.


If you email us, we receive your email address and whatever you include so we can respond. Do not send private keys, credentials, or raw private task contents.


Contact


For privacy questions or requests, email violet@briarwood.ai.

Published document text
Service terms
https://767-2676.com/terms
True Witness Here
767-2676.com https://767-2676.com/
Service terms
767-2676.com provides signed temporal evidence. These terms describe the scope published in its service offer and witness protocol.
What you receive
A signed time response states the service’s time and validity window. A digest witness records when the supplied SHA-256 commitment was presented, together with the submitted nonce and optional predecessor digest.
Your data
Keep the original task and schedule bytes with you. The witness endpoint accepts the digest and the fields defined by its request schema. It rejects additional fields.
Payment
Each signed time response or digest witness costs 0.001 USDC on Base through x402. Read the current payment challenge before authorizing a payment. Public documentation, verification keys, and saved examples are free to read.
Using the evidence
Verify the signature, expected digest, nonce, predecessor where applicable, and time window before relying on a response. Apply your own task authority and boundary conditions. The signed scope covers temporal evidence; identity, authorization, execution, delivery, and outcome require their own evidence.
Network delay and clock uncertainty remain part of verification. A saved sample retains its original timestamp and validity window.

PROCEED and STOP are the example consumer policy at the recorded checkpoint. These receipts are historical. They do not authorize a task now or prove that the task was carried out.
The receipt records that POPCORN witnessed a digest at its stated time. The stated time interval is signed, but its clock accuracy is not independently established here. Bundled historical public keys make these records reproducible; real use requires an independently trusted issuer key.
The current verifier requires the whole witness interval inside the saved task window. Crossing a boundary requires a recheck and stays STOP on this page. A changed task or failed signature also returns STOP. No wallet, payment, task execution, or data submission is started by the sliders.

True Witness Here — FreeTSA + POPCORN checkpoint
https://767-2676.com/evidence/freetsa-popcorn-2026-10-14/README.md
True Witness Here — FreeTSA + POPCORN checkpoint


Two native signed attestations bind to the same exact 385-byte checkpoint file. The checkpoint was witnessed on October 14, 2026; this verification package was prepared October 16, 2026 (Pacific).


Run both verifications


Extract the ZIP into a writable folder. Install Node.js 20 or newer and OpenSSL 3.x, then run from the extracted folder:


node verify.mjs



The checker makes no network requests and no payments. It reads the original evidence without altering it. It writes fresh verification.json and verification.log results beside the script, replacing the included results when rerun. A passing run exits with status 0 and reports "status": "PASS" with 12 passing checks.


OpenSSL must be available as openssl, or set the OPENSSL environment variable to its executable path. On Windows, the checker also recognizes Git for Windows' OpenSSL at C:/Program Files/Git/usr/bin/openssl.exe.


$env:OPENSSL = 'C:/Program Files/Git/usr/bin/openssl.exe'
node verify.mjs



Exact underlying commands


The log contains the commands and their output from the published run:


openssl version
openssl ts -verify -data checkpoint.txt -in checkpoint.tsr -CAfile freetsa-cacert.pem -untrusted freetsa-tsa.crt
openssl ts -reply -in checkpoint.tsr -text



The timestamp verification returned Verification: OK. To inspect the file digest separately:


openssl dgst -sha256 checkpoint.txt



The expected SHA-256 is:


03eecd1ed8866d816e183834fbf7f94543ecca18d0a1adc0f3b798252e6058a2



For POPCORN, verify.mjs uses Node's crypto.verify with SHA-256, the published P-256 key, and the 64-byte IEEE-P1363 signature. It verifies the exact ASCII JWS signing input (encodedHeader.encodedPayload), requires ES256 and the expected key ID, checks the separately pinned key thumbprint, compares the complete outer witness_receipt to the signed JSON payload, and compares the signed payload digest to the file's SHA-256. The script also checks the stated interval and evidence scope.


Files and results






File
Purpose





checkpoint.txt
Original 385-byte checkpoint. Preserve its exact bytes and line endings.




checkpoint.tsr
Original 4,643-byte RFC 3161 timestamp response.




popcorn-receipt.json
Original 7,019-byte full POPCORN response, including its ES256 compact JWS.




freetsa-cacert.pem
FreeTSA root certificate, obtained separately from its official HTTPS site.




freetsa-tsa.crt
FreeTSA TSA certificate, obtained separately from its official HTTPS site.




popcorn-keys.json
Public POPCORN key set, obtained separately from its issuer HTTPS endpoint.




verify.mjs
Portable offline checker with recorded trust fingerprints.




verification.json
Published verification results, input hashes and stated trust scope.




verification.log
Published OpenSSL commands and output.




operator-working.txt
Operator's published working, as supplied by Violet.




SHA256SUMS.txt
Package file hashes, for checking byte consistency.

    • 



Certificates and public keys were retrieved on October 16, 2026 (Pacific), separately from the receipt. The checker's trust starts with those issuer HTTPS endpoints and the fingerprints pinned in the script:


    

  • FreeTSA root certificate SHA-256 over DER bytes: a6379e7cecc05faa3cbf076013d745e327bbbaa38c0b9af22469d4701d18aabc.
    • 

  • POPCORN popcorn-witness-2026-01 RFC 7638 JWK SHA-256 thumbprint, base64url: ns4g75U5Bsn2bA3XtaSuQhDg065PVkhYgs3KOtJVPf0.
    • 



The package does not independently establish historical key ownership. A reader must decide whether to trust the stated sources and fingerprints; a package cannot establish that trust merely by including its own keys and checker. Certificate validity is evaluated by OpenSSL at the time of a rerun, so a future run can fail if certificates have expired.


Exact scope


The result establishes two valid signatures binding the same file digest under the stated keys. Busindre's independent confirmation on October 15 covered the FreeTSA timestamp/checkpoint match. It did not cover the POPCORN signature; that signature is checked by the portable script in this package.


FreeTSA's accuracy field is unspecified. No policy-supplied clock bound was verified. Strict-deadline reasoning from the FreeTSA side therefore remains indeterminate in this package. POPCORN's ±10-second interval is issuer-asserted, not an independently measured UTC error bound.


The checks do not validate the truth of the checkpoint's contents, prove action execution, grant authorization, or establish delivery. No certificate revocation checks or original .tsq request nonce comparison were performed. The package does not independently validate payment settlement, nonce freshness, or clock accuracy.

Published document text
Lab record, 31 August 2026 | POPCORN
https://767-2676.com/lab-record

  

    

      767-2676.com https://767-2676.com/
      POPCORN
    

    

      

        
Cross-model x402 payment lab record, 31 August 2026

        
On this date two models from different companies, Grok and Codex, coordinated one live payment to this service with nothing between them but text I copied and pasted. This is the full record: what each model did, the exact bytes, the transaction, the refusal, and the parts of the Codex transcript that show the approval and the decision not to pay a second time. It claims one settlement and one refusal, nothing more.

        
Read it, verify the packet yourself without a wallet, and tell me what's wrong with it.

        

          PDF https://767-2676.com/lab-record.pdf·
          Markdown https://github.com/violetclaire/popcorn-temporal-anchor/blob/main/docs/lab-record-2026-08-31.md·
          Verify the packet https://github.com/violetclaire/popcorn-temporal-anchor/blob/main/examples/witness/evaluation-packet.proceed-002.production.json·
          Transaction on Base https://basescan.org/tx/0x477e726933c94ccad5682d03ecee4f3d5bb618387ac7437fc817bdd2fe946e5c
        

      

      

        
One settlement and one refusal

        
In this operator-recorded test, Grok prepared an exact 228-byte replacement schedule after the original schedule expired. Violet Herod carried the protocol text between Grok and Codex by copy and paste. Codex reconstructed the bytes, checked the digest and live public state, and requested approval before the payment.

        
The approved payment was 0.001 USDC on Base. POPCORN issued a signed witness receipt for schedule 002. A later proposal carried a different digest without the exact bytes behind it; Codex declined the second payment.

        
Verify the exact artifact

        
Schedule 002 SHA-256, encoded as base64url:

        
kvYtty3Ifr6SMK8xU76uP0OowwGRCELi6nufb1hWh3M

        
The public PROCEED packet https://github.com/violetclaire/popcorn-temporal-anchor/blob/main/examples/witness/evaluation-packet.proceed-002.production.json contains the schedule bytes, digest, signed receipt, public verification key, settlement reference, and a one-byte tamper case. Verification of the saved packet requires no new payment. See the full text record and packet-specific verification command https://github.com/violetclaire/popcorn-temporal-anchor/blob/main/docs/lab-record-2026-08-31.md, or inspect the recorded transaction on Base https://basescan.org/tx/0x477e726933c94ccad5682d03ecee4f3d5bb618387ac7437fc817bdd2fe946e5c.

        
What the evidence means

        
The receipt provides signed evidence that a particular digest was presented within a recorded time interval. Checking the original bytes against the digest detects changes. The agent applies its own schedule and authorization rules.

        
This was one human-mediated test. It does not establish direct autonomous communication between the models, a general safety guarantee, task authorization, or proof of downstream execution.
